  - `src/game/Sprite.tsx`. One drawing function is shown outside the canvas, in garage cards, the store and leaderboard rows, instead of the same car being drawn a second way.
  - `src/game/chainBoard.ts` (game-board kit). It needs nothing from the MiniPay kit.
  - The MiniPay sign-in check leaves `src/game/leaderboard.ts` alone, because that file signs in only outside MiniPay.
- **Saved data reads clamp `limit` to 100** in `collection().list` (and so `useCollection`), for every app. Before, a board asking for 200 got an error.
- **Free play first** is now the opening rule of the game guide. The guide also asks for these, and closes with a checklist:
  - every obstacle, power-up and character the plan names, each with its own name in the code;
  - the first obstacle within 3 seconds;
  - NPCs that signal before they act;
  - power-ups with a visible timer;
  - the score drawn on the canvas.
- **Run check (D67):**
  - For a 2D game it looks at the start screen before pressing Play. It reports a canvas that is one flat colour, and a start screen with no Play or Start button (also "race").
  - The blank check after play now treats a one-colour canvas as blank, not only a see-through one.
  - With screenshots on, a 2D game's phone pictures are its start screen and a moment of play, then the computer width.
- **Review and visual check:** the review (D87) is told which picture is the start screen and which is play. The in-browser visual check (P17) is told it sees a game before Play, so it doesn't report obstacles, characters or a counting score missing. A request is a game by any of the project's own messages. Those false reports cost the owner a build each.
- **Plan (D32, D88):**
  - For a game, the planner always asks about obstacles and challenges, power-ups, and the computer's characters, each on its own multiple-choice card fitted to the game. If the model leaves one out, a generic card is added.
  - The model gets 5 cards. The server adds Leaderboard (skipped when the request already asks for one), MiniPay and Colours, so the most is 8. The owner didn't pick between 6 and 8; Claude went with the proposal's 8.
  - The model's score questions are dropped for games.
  - The planner also returns a game brief (name, pitch, loop, how a round ends, how it gets harder, two or three extras). The plan card shows it and the builder gets it as the spec. It can't be edited on the card yet: a later message changes it.
  - The game rules and the brief field go only with a game's request, so other plans cost what they did. The plan's output limit went from 1,500 to 2,000 tokens. The worst case is still inside the planning headroom (tested).
- **Checked:**
  - In a real headless browser, a lane racer written the new way passes the run check, with screenshots in the right order.
  - The same game without `onIdleFrame` is caught, and so is a start screen with no Play button.
  - The contract compiles and its tests pass.
  - A MiniPay game passing `gameBoard(GameBoardAbi, GameBoardAddress[chain.id])` passes the app check, the MiniPay checks and the type check. Forgetting it is caught.
  - The runner turns `game-board` on for "A snake game for MiniPay with a leaderboard", and not without MiniPay.
  - Planner cards, brief and costs are tested.
  - The whole test suite passes.
- **UNVERIFIED:**
  - Any of this with a real model: whether DeepSeek writes fitting obstacle, power-up and character cards, and a good brief.
  - The leaderboard on a published app with a real wallet signature.
  - A real MiniPay post: fee-currency payment for a contract call, and how MiniPay shows it.
  - The plan card's look in the workspace (only tested in jsdom).
- **Not in this change:** Phases C (building blocks and the game probe), D (a bot that plays, and run-check tests of the leaderboard and wallet), E (shop contract) and F (extras) of the plan. B4 (checking that the planned obstacles appear) needs the probe from Phase C.

### D94. Games are played and tested: building blocks, a game probe, and a run check that plays, posts a score and connects a wallet (phases C and D of GAMES_PLAN.md; numbered D93 at first, renumbered because main took D92 for OKX Wallet)
- Date: 11 Nov 2026 · Asked for by: the owner ("go ahead with phase C and D") · Built by: Claude.
- **Building blocks** (new kit files in `src/game/`, source in `kits/game-blocks.ts`). No packages, and nothing is allocated while the game runs. The guide has a section on each and asks the builder to use them instead of writing its own:
  - `world.ts`: `Pool`, `Spawner` (a short grace before the first spawn, faster with difficulty, never closer than its fastest gap), `difficulty(seconds)`, `lanes`, `pick`.
  - `npc.ts`: one `Actor` shape for `traffic`, `oncoming`, `chaser`, `patrol`, `shooter` and `rival`. Each sets `warn` from 0 to 1 before it acts, so a player can react. Also `hitBox` (a little smaller than what is drawn) and `offscreen`.
  - `powerups.ts`: `Effects` (activate, isActive, remaining, fraction, update with an end callback), with colours for shield, magnet, slow, double, boost and life.
  - `hud.ts`: `drawHud` (the score panel, distance, best, lives, combo, a shrinking bar per power-up) and `drawBanner`.
  - `haptics.ts`: `buzz`, which the mute button also turns off.
- **The game probe** (`src/game/probe.ts`). The game calls `declareGame({ obstacles, powerups, npcs })` once, `spawned(kind)` when one is created, and `probe(score, x, y, speed)` each frame. `GameCanvas` reports rounds: one starts when the game runs, and ends when it pauses or the canvas leaves the page. All of it returns at once unless the run check turned it on (`window.__gbGame`), so real players never pay for it. A file check asks for all three calls in any project with a `GameCanvas`.
- **Checking is faster than real time.** While the check runs, `GameCanvas` runs 4 game steps per screen frame, so about 7 seconds of play cover about 28 seconds of game. The probe keeps its own clock, so a game that mounts a fresh canvas each round is timed right. The first moments of play run at normal speed, so the blank-canvas check and the review's "playing" picture see what a player sees.
- **The run check plays a 2D game** (new `game-check.ts`; not for the review's screenshot run):
  - **The bot.** It presses Play with no wallet, then taps both sides and presses the keys for about 7 seconds. After each game over it presses Play again and checks a new round starts.
  - **What it reports to the builder's fix loop:**
    - a score that never rises;
    - no obstacle or character within 3 seconds of Play;
    - a declared kind that never appeared in a round of 15 seconds or more;
    - controls that never moved the player (x or y);
    - a game that didn't get harder (the speed passed to `probe` never rose) in a round of 10 seconds or more;
    - no Play again button, or one that doesn't start a round;
    - no `probe` calls at all.
  - **It stays silent when it can't tell.** If the bot never survived long enough, it writes a note rather than an issue, because a false finding costs a fix.
- **With a stand-in wallet** (a second page with `window.ethereum` that says yes and logs what it was asked):
  - If the page has a Connect wallet button, pressing it must ask the wallet for an account and then show the short address.
  - If the game shows `PostScore`, the check plays until a game over, posts a score as "Check Bot", and needs both "You're on the board!" and the name in the leaderboard list. This runs on the preview store, because the run check's build has no data server; the data rule itself is the file check from D93.
- **As MiniPay** (a third page, only with the MiniPay kit): a visible Connect wallet button is reported, and so is any signature request. Play must still work.
- **Time.** A game's run check may take up to 50 seconds (other apps keep 25). Measured locally, a game with a leaderboard took 12 to 15 seconds per check.
- **Planned kinds (B4)** are tested through `declareGame`: the guide tells the builder to declare the kinds the plan named, with the same short names. Nothing yet compares the plan's wording with the declared names.
- **Changed from the plan.** There is no forced game over (`endRound`): a game that never ends during the check simply isn't judged on Play again. The leaderboard is tested on the preview store rather than an in-memory server that applies the project's rules, which the file check already covers.
- **Games already built** get the new kit files (they are added, never overwritten). Their next build will be asked to add `declareGame`, `spawned` and `probe`, which costs one fix round.
- **Checked:**
  - Unit tests for every rule, including when it stays silent (`game-check.test.ts`).
  - In a real headless browser:
    - a racer built only from the blocks passes the type check and the run check, meeting every kind it declared;
    - a game with no obstacles, score, control or rising speed gets all five findings;
    - a game over with no Play again is caught;
    - posting a score with the stand-in wallet shows it on the board;
    - a `PostScore` wired to a different board than the one on screen is caught;
    - a MiniPay game is opened as MiniPay, and a Connect wallet button there is caught.
  - The game tests passed twice in a row, and the whole suite passes.
- **UNVERIFIED:** a real model writing games with the blocks and the probe; how often the random bot survives long enough to judge everything in real builds; the extra check time on the production checker; real wallets and real MiniPay.

### D95. Games sell things safely and give people a reason to come back: a tested G$ shop, and the extras (phases E and F of GAMES_PLAN.md)
- Date: 11 Nov 2026 · Asked for by: the owner ("go ahead with phase E and F", with the Neon Drift transcript, whose last requests were to put Play again and Post score on the game screen, show the top three racers on the race screen, and show distance and coins on the game-over panel) · Built by: Claude.
- **The shop is a kit, `game-shop`, with a tested contract.** Before, the builder wrote a shop contract for each game. Neon Drift's "RaceShop" was one, and it cost a deploy.
  - `contracts/GameShop.sol` sells three kinds of item, each paid in G$ with one `transferAndCall` (no separate approve step):
    - unique items (a car, a skin), owned once;
    - counted items (level ups, power packs with `perPurchase`);
    - the season pass, which belongs to the current season and ends with it. `startSeason(endsAt)` starts the next one, and earlier passes stop counting.
  - The price must be exact, so nobody overpays and a refused purchase costs nothing.
  - The owner sets the price list in one `setItems` transaction, can pause, and withdraws the takings. `Ownable2Step` and `ReentrancyGuard` protect it. Only the G$ token can report a payment.
  - `contracts/test/GameShop.t.sol` has 10 tests, including attacks: a stranger changing prices, withdrawing, starting a season or pausing; calling the payment hook directly to get an item free; and moving G$ out.
  - The shop is not mistaken for a vault (`rolesOf`), because players buy rather than deposit and withdraw.
- **The game owns its item list.** `export const SHOP: ShopItem[]` in the game's code: ids, names, kinds, prices in G$, and details.
  - `useShop(SHOP, GameShopAbi, GameShopAddress[chain.id])` reads the on-chain prices, what the player owns and holds, the pass and the season end. It buys, and gives the owner a setup action.
  - `<Store>` shows each item with the game's own drawing (Sprite), its price, Buy, Owned or Use, how many the player has, the season end and the G$ balance.
  - After deploying, the owner sees "Put these items on sale": one transaction with every price from the code.
  - Free starter items ("0") are owned by everyone with no wallet, so the game stays fully playable for free.
  - The guide asks for fair play: bought items are cosmetic or a capped convenience, never what decides a board with prizes.
  - It also asks for `{"name": "GameShop", "args": {"gd_": "${GOODDOLLAR}"}}` in `contracts/deploy.json`, and an owner-only control to start a season. The game-board guide now names its own deploy.json line too.
- **When the shop turns on.** The runner turns `game-shop` on for a 2D game whose request or plan answers say it sells things: store, shop, season pass, garage, level ups, skins, buying cars or items. It never turns on from those words alone, because a bookshop isn't a game. For the owner's exact Neon Drift request, the runner turns on `game-2d`, `mini-app`, `game-board` and `game-shop` together (tested).
- **MiniPay.** `src/game/chainTx.ts` (`sendGameTx`) sends a game's contract calls. In a browser it's a normal transaction. Inside MiniPay it pays the network fee in the first dollar coin the player has, as a legacy transaction. The owner tested G$ inside MiniPay on a phone and confirmed it works (11 Nov 2026), which answers GAMES_PLAN E3. GoodBuild's own automated checks still use a stand-in MiniPay.
- **Extras** (`src/game/extras.ts`):
  - near misses (`nearMiss`) and a `Combo` multiplier;
  - `coins` earned by playing, kept on the device;
  - `Missions`: three a day, the same for everyone, tracked by stat, some within one run. Finished missions add season XP, and `seasonTier(xp, tiers, hasPass)` gives the tier, its progress, and which rewards are unlocked, with pass-only tiers;
  - a daily course (`dailySeed`, `seededRandom`);
  - a `Ghost` of the best run;
  - `firstTime` for a one-run tip;
  - `shareScore` (the phone's share sheet, or a copy);
  - `prefersReducedMotion`.
  - The planner's brief picks the extras for a game, and the guide asks for every one the brief names.
- **On-screen pieces:**
  - `GameOver` is a panel over the bottom of the game: the score, "New best!", the best, the run's numbers (distance, coins), a big Play again, Share, and `PostScore` inside it. This is what the owner asked for after scrolling to find Play again.
  - `TopThree` shows the board's top three on the race screen.
  - `GameSettings` has sound and vibration switches. Vibration now has its own setting as well as following mute.
  - `GameCanvas` gets `onHidden`, so a phone call or tab switch pauses the run behind a Resume button instead of carrying on.
- **The run check** (D94) now reports a Play again that sits below the screen after a game over, which the player has to scroll to find.
- **Checked:**
  - The shop contract compiles and all 10 of its tests pass in the local EVM.
  - A game with the store passes the app check and the type check, with and without MiniPay.
  - A game with every extra passes the type check and the run check in a real browser.
  - The game tests now use the on-game panel and the top three, and a game with Play again below a scoring card is caught.
  - The runner turns the shop on for the Neon Drift request and not for a game that sells nothing.
  - The full suite passes.
- **UNVERIFIED:**
  - A real model building a game with the shop and extras.
  - The owner's setup on a deployed `GameShop` (G$ inside MiniPay itself was tested on a phone by the owner).
  - Whether DeepSeek keeps purchases fair as the guide asks.

### D97. Credits are shown as credits, not dollars: 1 credit = $0.01 (display only; amends D51)

- **Decided by:** the owner, 2026-11-12.
- **Why:** a dollar balance invites people to compare every build against other tools' prices and feels stingier than a count of credits. Credits are our own unit, so a promo or a cost change moves what a build costs in credits, not what the balance "is in dollars".
- **What changed:** screens show whole credits (100 credits = $1): the balance pills and nav hint, the Credits page, Add credits (amounts, presets and the "plus bonus" line), the top-up pill, the per-build estimate and "N credits of AI" in the chat, and the free-start copy (20 credits free, 50 more when verified). `src/lib/credits-math.ts` has `MICRO_PER_CREDIT`, `microToCredits`, `formatCredits` and `formatCreditsShort`; they replace `formatMicroUsd`, `formatUsd` and the inline `toFixed(2)` calls.
- **What did not change:** the ledger, quotes, holds, caps, the contract and billing all stay in micro-USD (D51). Nothing is stored in credits.
- **Left as dollars on purpose:** the naira buy dialog's fee lines (they are real money), blog posts, and `CAP_MESSAGE` in `server/credits/cap.ts` (a test pins it to the earlier wording; change both together later).

### D98. Hackathon page at /hackathon; credit grants keyed to the GoodDollar root (proposal)

- **Decided by:** the owner for the page and its look (approved the poster design and asked for the `/hackathon` route, 12 Nov 2026). The credit rules are Claude's proposal, waiting on the owner (docs/HACKATHON.md §4).
- **Page:** a public route, reusing sign-in and GoodDollar verification. `useGoodIdVerify` was taken out of `VerifyCard` so both use the same flow. Styles are plain global CSS prefixed `hk-`, because the Turbopack CSS rule in `next.config.ts` turns CSS modules off. It's a louder take on the D12 tokens (poster hero, marquee, big display numbers), kept to violet, pink and Unbounded.
- **Credit grants (not built):** one ledger entry per GoodDollar root (`hackathon-2027:<root>` as the idempotency key), the chain re-read at claim time, credits only spendable on GoodBuild AI, paid in two parts, expiring at the deadline, with a total budget. Details and limits: docs/HACKATHON.md §2.
- **Checked:** type check, lint, the content test (the prizes add up to the pool) and the VerifyCard tests pass. The page was opened in a local dev server at 1440px and 390px, with no sideways scroll.
- **UNVERIFIED:** a real face check from this page, signed-in states on the live site, and the hackathon's dates, prize split and partner details.

### D99. Hackathon credits: 50 per builder in two parts, one grant per GoodDollar root

- **Decided by:** the owner on 12 Nov 2026: the two-part option, about 100 builders, 50 credits each (down from 500, which a ~$30 budget couldn't cover). The 20 + 30 split is Claude's choice.
- **What:**
  - 20 credits on registering.
  - 30 more after the first deploy on Celo or Celo Sepolia made after registering.
  - A 5,000-credit total cap (`HACKATHON_BUDGET_CREDITS`).
  - Claims close on 8 Feb 2027 (DRAFT) and can be paused with `HACKATHON_CLAIMS=off`.
  - Hackathon builders top up like anyone else: the grant is an `adjust` entry on the same ledger balance.
- **Against abuse:**
  - The root is read from the chain at claim time.
  - It's unique in `hackathon_registrations`, and the ledger idempotency keys are built from it.
  - Budget and grants run under one advisory lock.
  - The deploy part counts only server-verified deployments.
- **Checked:** five claim tests on PGlite: unverified refused, replay pays once, second account on the same root refused, deploy part only after a later Celo deploy, and budget, deadline and off switch. Also the API and proxy tests and the type check.
- **UNVERIFIED:** a real claim on the live site with a verified wallet, and the race tests on real Postgres (run with `TEST_DATABASE_URL`).
- **Not built:** expiry of unused grants, and an admin view.

### D100. Hackathon: unspent credits expire after the deadline; admin page at /hackathon/admin

- **Decided by:** the owner asked for both (12 Nov 2026). The rules below are Claude's.
- **Expiry:**
  - After `CLAIMS_CLOSE`, the standalone worker sweeps every hour, and admins can run it from the page.
  - Usage since registering counts against the grant first, so only the unspent part is removed. Bought credits are never touched, and no balance goes below zero.
  - Each person is expired once (ledger key by root). A sweep that races a spend leaves that row for the next run.
- **Admin page:**
  - Wallets in `ADMIN_WALLETS` only; anyone else gets a 404. It's also in the proxy's signed-in pages.
  - Shows totals, budget used, registrations per day, and every builder.
  - Flags accounts made under 10 minutes before claiming, and wallets linked to another GoodDollar identity.
  - Lists refused claims (same identity on another account, or budget spent), now kept in `hackathon_refusals` (migration 0018, regenerated before merge).
- **Checked:** the expiry tests (refuses before close, removes only the unspent grant, keeps bought credits, runs once) and the report tests (totals, new-wallet flag, refused second claim, per-day count), on PGlite. Also the type check and lint.
- **UNVERIFIED:**
  - The admin page rendered with a real admin session.
  - The worker's hourly sweep on the server: watch for `[worker] hackathon expiry:` lines after the deadline.
  - Only the standalone worker (`pnpm worker`) sweeps, not the web process's inline worker.

### D101. Hackathon entries start on GoodBuild; GoodBuild is linked as a partner

- **Decided by:** the owner, 12 Nov 2026.
- **Rule:** every entry is started on GoodBuild, so its first commit comes from GoodBuild. After that, builders may connect GitHub (D96) and keep building outside.
- **Shown on `/hackathon`:** in the rules and the FAQ. Before this, the FAQ said entries didn't have to use GoodBuild.
- **Not enforced in code yet:** submissions aren't built. When they are, check that the entry's repo is one GoodBuild created through GitHub sync, and that its first commit is GoodBuild's.
- **Partner card:** the GoodBuild card links to https://goodbuildit.xyz/ instead of "Link coming soon".

### D102. Plan mode gets a plan card with Build this plan, and typed text answers an open question

- **Decided by:** the owner, 13 Nov 2026 ("the plan toggle, do steps 1 to 3 and 5 first"). Claude proposed the design.
- **Problem:** Plan mode produced a paragraph and a one-line "Plan" step row. `record.plan` was never drawn, and to act on a plan the person had to flip the toggle to Build and type something.
- **Plan card:** a plan turn shows its summary and numbered steps (`PlanDraftCard`, same card style as the questions summary). Build-mode turns don't show it, because their steps are already in the step rows.
- **Actions, on the newest reply only (older plans are read-only, like the questions card):**
  - **Build this plan** sends a build turn whose message is the plan itself (`planBuildPrompt`), so the builder, the history window and the rolling memory all see it with no server change. It sends `skipPlan`, so a first message that was planned in Plan mode builds instead of asking the first-message questions.
  - **Change something** switches the composer to Plan mode and puts the cursor in it. The next plan reply replaces this one.
- **Typed answers:** while the questions card has an unanswered question, text typed in the composer in Build mode (300 characters or fewer) is saved as that question's "Other" answer. Before, sending ended the card and started a build. Start building is still the way to build from the card. Longer text, and anything sent in Plan mode, is sent as a normal message.
- **Plan mode asking questions:** done in D103.
- **Checked:** `plan-card.test.tsx` (card, actions, double-tap guard, build-mode turns show no card, prompt text), lint, and the unchanged type-error count. **UNVERIFIED:** the composer behaviour (`chat-panel.tsx`, no test file exists for it), a real model's `set_plan` output in the card, and a phone.
- **Known limit:** if someone has stepped Back to an earlier question, a typed answer goes to the next unanswered one, not the one on screen.

### D103. Plan mode can ask its own questions before it plans (extends D102)

- **Decided by:** the owner, 13 Nov 2026 ("yes, do step 4"). Claude proposed the design.
- **What it does:** in Plan mode on a project, the model may call `ask_before_planning` with one to three questions when the request leaves out something that changes the plan. They show on the same card as the first-message questions. The turn ends there, with no further model call.
- **Finishing the card:** the last screen says **Draft the plan** and Skip says "Skip, just plan it". Both send a Plan mode message ("Draft the plan with my answers"). The answers reach that turn through the same `planBrief` block every turn already gets, so there is no new plumbing. Typed text in the composer answers the open question while Plan mode is selected (Build mode, for the first-message questions).
- **Cap:** never twice in a row. The tool is offered only when the newest reply in the chat isn't a set of questions (`askedJustBefore`), so one round per plan. The choice is saved on the turn's setup (`canAsk`), so a resumed turn sees the same tools.
- **Not the first-message planner:** that one (D32) is unchanged. A project's first message still gets its own questions, which include colours, MiniPay and game cards. Plan mode's round has none of those.
- **Why a different tool name:** `ask_questions` marks the first-message planning call in the demo model and the tests, so Plan mode's tool is `ask_before_planning`.
- **Cost:** a question round is a normal Plan mode turn that stops after its first model call, and the plan after it is a second Plan mode turn. No extra call beyond those two. A model that asks when it didn't need to costs one turn, so the prompt tells it to skip questions when the request is clear.
- **Checked:** parsing (three cards, no colour card), the turn ends after one call, the answer reaches the next plan, no second round in a row, an unusable call is refused and the model plans instead, build mode never gets the tool, and the card's labels. Lint and the type-error count (unchanged).
- **UNVERIFIED:** whether a real model asks well or too often (watch how many Plan mode turns end in questions), the composer's typed answers, and a phone.

### D104. The visual check says why it has no result, and doesn't report a skip as a failure

- **Decided by:** Claude, 13 Nov 2026, from a tester's report ("The visual check couldn't run for this build").
- **Found:** that line showed in three cases that aren't faults. (1) A project on the own build (games, 3D, or a Sandpack fallback) can't be screenshotted from the browser, and the workspace reported that skip as "couldn't run" on every such build the review (D87) hadn't looked at. (2) Someone out of free AI or credit got a 402 from the check, shown the same way. The review is also skipped at the free ceiling, so a second project near the cap hits this. (3) A long page full of photos can make a PNG bigger than the 4 MB the route takes, which failed zod with a 400.
- **Now:** the own build clears the pending check and shows nothing. A 402 says no AI credit is left for the check; a 413 says the page was too large. The preview sends a JPEG (quality 0.85) when the PNG data URL is over 5 MB, and the route and `runVisualCheck` take PNG or JPEG. Anything else still says "couldn't run".
- **Checked:** unit tests for the messages and a JPEG check; DeepSeek's vision call answered live with thinking off. **UNVERIFIED:** in a real browser.

### D105. One wallet sign-in per app: useDbSession() is shared across components

- **Decided by:** Claude, 13 Nov 2026, from a tester's report ("wasn't able to test it or even get in": Receipt Sheet, a private sheet behind Sign in with wallet).
- **Found:** each `useDbSession()` call kept its own copy of the session, with nothing telling the others when it changed. The app-data guide tells the builder to put "a big button calling useDbSession().signIn()" on its own card or page, so the page that decides what to show reads one copy and the button signs in another. The sign-in worked, but the page never changed, in the preview and once published. Reproduced in a real browser: the page stayed on the sign-in card after the click.
- **Now:** `src/lib/useDb.ts` keeps one session for the app (a small store read with `useSyncExternalStore`, as the wallet layer does). The first `useDbSession()` reads it and watches for a wallet switch, once; `signingIn` and `error` stay with the component whose button was pressed. The guide says any component may call it. The file is platform-owned, so a published app gets it on its next publish, and a project's preview after its next turn.
- **Checked:** `db-session.test.ts` builds an app shaped like the report and, in a real browser, signs in from a child card (the page opens) and signs out from the header (the card comes back). It is skipped without the browser, like the run check's tests. Lint, types, full suite.

### D106. Credits scan keeps its own checkpoint, and a paid purchase can always be recovered (GB-01)

- **Decided by:** the owner, 13 Nov 2026 ("yes, go ahead"), on Claude's plan, after GoodDollar's Daybreak Blue security review (finding GB-01, Medium).
- **Found (confirmed in source):** `syncRedemptions` started each scan at the newest stored `Redeemed` event. (1) An empty stretch longer than one call (50,000 blocks) never moved that start, so every later tick re-read the same empty window. (2) A newer purchase reported directly by its browser moved the start past an older one whose tab had closed, so the older one was never scanned. Either way the G$ was paid and the credit didn't land on its own. The dialog also promised "reopen this in a minute and it will be credited", which reopening didn't do, and a server error after payment said "Nothing was charged".
- **Now:** a `credits_sync_cursor` table (migration 0019) holds the last block fully read per chain. The scan starts there (or `CREDITS_START_BLOCK_<chain>` on its first run), re-reads 64 blocks behind it for reorgs, moves it after every 2,000-block chunk that was fully recorded, and never moves it backward (`greatest`, so two servers can't undo each other). Direct receipts no longer affect where the scan reads. Events still credit exactly once (unique on chain, tx, log index; ledger idempotency key).
- **Heals the past:** the first scan after deploy starts at `CREDITS_START_BLOCK_42220` (82304791, 9 Nov) and re-reads all history once, about 7 one-minute ticks, crediting anything missed. If the variable isn't set the scan starts at block 0 (about a day on mainnet); the sync now logs a warning when that happens.
- **In the browser:** the transaction hash is saved to localStorage the moment the wallet returns it, re-sent on the next page load, and forgotten once the server has read it. Once G$ has moved, the dialog says the payment went through, shows the full transaction hash, and never says "nothing was charged". A **Missing a payment?** form takes a pasted hash and calls the existing `/api/credits/redeem`, which credits whoever the on-chain event names, so it's safe to expose.
- **Evidence:** `scripts/credits-reconcile.ts` (read-only) prints, per chain, the checkpoint and its lag behind the head, events with no ledger entry, a recent transaction-to-ledger sample, and any event with more than one ledger entry.
- **Checked:** `onchain.test.ts`: an empty gap over 50,000 blocks then a payment; an older unreported payment after a newer direct receipt; exactly once across overlapping scans, re-reads and a receipt; the checkpoint stays at the last full chunk when the node fails; never moves backward. `pending-payments.test.ts`: save, expiry, server answers, and retry-on-load forgetting only settled payments. Lint, types, full suite. The reconcile script ran against a local database and live Celo Sepolia.
- **Verified on production (13 Nov 2026, owner ran `credits-reconcile.ts`, read-only):** Celo 42220, contract `0x800A…e8c2F`, start block 82304791. The first scan caught up from the deploy block: checkpoint 82703297 against head 82703309 (12 blocks behind). 9 `Redeemed` events, all 9 credited one-to-one to a ledger entry (3.835 USD), 0 uncredited, 0 duplicate ledger entries. No missed payment was found in history. 23 quotes issued, 8 marked paid: the 15 unpaid expired with no G$ moved; the ninth event has no quote row because it is the launch test purchase: tx `0xc0717ff2fce58f248a0025f06d1c208414400c44e3329c4cbbb00ecae720e492` (block 82309137, 0.25 USD), a G$ `transferAndCall` from test wallet `0x5ac1…8c19` (not the owner, not a `grant`), made by `scripts/credits-live-purchase.mjs`, which signs its own quote and never writes `credit_quotes`. It is credited once like the rest. Read through the RPC by the owner; not yet viewed on Celoscan.
- **UNVERIFIED:** the dialog (paid state, Missing a payment?) in a real browser and on a phone.
- **Superseded in part (D111, 14 Nov 2026):** "re-reads 64 blocks behind it for reorgs" no longer holds. The scan now reads only to head − 30 (confirmation depth), the checkpoint never passes head − 1,800, and each scan re-reads the newest 1,800 blocks. "Events still credit exactly once (… ledger idempotency key)" now means once per quote ref, not per transaction and log index (GB-13).

### D107. Mainnet checks find money-holding contracts by what they do, and a mainnet record needs a passed gate (GB-02)

- **Decided by:** the owner, 13 Nov 2026 ("yes, go ahead with your recommendations"), on Claude's plan, after GoodDollar's Daybreak Blue security review (finding GB-02, Medium, conditional).
- **Found (confirmed in source):** the mainnet exploit gate ran only for the DAO/vault kit or for source text matching `deposit…`/`withdraw…` or `propose…`/`vote…` (`rolesOf`). A G$ pool with `joinPool`/`exitPool` skipped it, and comments could switch it on or off. The deploy compile re-ran the AST guardrails only for upgradeable projects. Not in the review: `recordDeployment` checked the bytecode against the project but not that the code had passed prepare's gate, so code compiled and deployed around GoodBuild could still be recorded and published.
- **Now:**
  - **Deploy-time guardrails:** `compileForDeploy` runs the security guardrails (`guardrailIssues(..., { securityOnly: true })`: delegatecall/callcode, selfdestruct, assembly, tx.origin, low-level call with data, proxy/Multicall imports) on every contract on every chain. House style (require strings, a token calling its own transfer) stays in the builder loop, so a safe older project still deploys.
  - **Money-holding detection from the AST** (`src/server/agent/custody.ts`): a payable function, `receive`, a payable `fallback`, a `transferFrom`/`safeTransferFrom` on another token, a token-received hook (`onTokenTransfer`, `onERC721Received`, `onERC1155Received`, `onERC1155BatchReceived`, `tokensReceived`), or an ERC4626/Governor base. A payable constructor and `super.`/`this.` calls don't count. Deliberately broad.
  - **What it requires:** a known shape (`rolesOf` matches) keeps its full exploit-test set. Any other money-holding contract needs `testExploitUnauthorizedCall` and `testExploitReentrancy` at the mainnet gate, where every test runs. **Not in the builder loop** (owner's choice, 13 Nov 2026: "Mainnet gate only"): requiring them there made every G$ tip jar, shop or game, and the demo tip jar, write two extra tests before a testnet build passed. A mainnet deploy without them stops with "Ask the builder to fix them". `checkContracts` reports the custody list but doesn't require anything from it. On mainnet it also needs the owner's review: prepare answers `needs_owner_review` with where the code takes money in, and the deploy modal shows that list and a checkbox ("I've reviewed where money goes in and out, and I understand GoodBuild hasn't audited this contract") before it prepares again with `ownerReviewed`.
  - **Records need a passed gate:** every successful mainnet prepare writes a `deploy_reviews` row (migration 0020) for the project, chain and exact source hash: exploit tests passed or not required, the custody list, the wallet, and when the owner reviewed. `recordDeployment` on mainnet refuses (`not_reviewed`) without it. The table is the audit trail for the reviewer's evidence item 3.
  - **Wording:** the gate's error says passing isn't an audit; the deploy summary on mainnet adds "Passing GoodBuild's checks isn't an audit" under Unaudited; a failed gate offers "Ask the builder to fix them".
- **Trade-off:** on mainnet, simple contracts that take money (a payable tip jar, a shop that pulls G$ with `transferFrom`) now need the two general exploit tests and one owner confirmation; the owner finds that out at deploy, not while building. A false positive costs a confirmation; a false negative let a money-holding contract skip its checks.
- **No row at record time** (prepared before this release, or deployed around GoodBuild): `recordDeployment` runs the gate itself, and records (writing the row) only if it passes and the code needs no owner review. A money-holding contract outside the known shapes that never had the owner's review is refused (`not_reviewed`), so the review can't be skipped.
- **Checked:** `gb02.test.ts`: the reviewer's regression (a `joinPool`/`exitPool` G$ pool with a delegatecall and no kit marker is refused at mainnet prepare, and at the testnet deploy compile); the same pool without exploit tests is refused naming both; with passing tests it needs the owner's review, then writes the audit row; a mainnet record of that pool without a passed prepare is refused and succeeds after it; a contract holding no money and never prepared is recorded after the gate runs at record time; a contract holding no money gets a row with nothing required; deploy-time guardrails block selfdestruct, tx.origin, assembly and a low-level call but not a require string; `custodyOf` finds each form and ignores a payable constructor, `super.transferFrom`, comments and names. `dao-vault.test.ts`: a known vault shape needs no review; a payable tip jar needs the general tests at the mainnet gate, and testnet builds are unchanged (the builder-loop tests for the demo tip jar pass as before). `deploy-modal.test.tsx`: the review step and the fix action. Lint, types, full suite.
- **UNVERIFIED:** the review step in a real browser and on a phone; a real model writing the general exploit tests for a non-vault contract.
- **Correction (D110, 14 Nov 2026):** this entry, the reply to the reviewer and the UI said the gate "runs every test" and that `exploit_tests = passed` meant the required exploit tests passed. That overstated it. The gate ran the tests, but "passed" was decided by a name match over the raw text of any `contracts/**/*.t.sol` file (comments included) and was recorded even when no test had run. A vault or DAO shape (`rolesOf`) was also decided on raw text, comments included. Money detection read only the contract's own functions and the written names of its bases. D110 fixes this; rows in `deploy_reviews` written before D110 still say `passed` on the old meaning.

### D108. A deploy transaction Celo hasn't confirmed is kept and checked, never re-signed blindly (GB-03)

- **Decided by:** the owner, 13 Nov 2026 ("yes you shall"), on Claude's plan, after GoodDollar's Daybreak Blue security review (finding GB-03, Low, conditional).
- **Found (confirmed in source):** when `waitForTransactionReceipt` timed out (120 seconds in the modal), `run.ts` deleted the saved transaction hash, in both the normal path and the upgradeable two-step path (`sendOnce`). Try again then asked the wallet for a new creation transaction. A timeout doesn't prove the first was dropped, so both could be mined: two contracts and two network fees. The step also showed as Failed with "Celo didn't answer in time, so nothing was sent and nothing was spent", which was wrong after a signature. Deploy transactions carry no value (`sendTransaction` has no `value`), so a duplicate costs gas only: no CELO or G$ goes into the second contract.
- **Now:**
  - **Kept on a timeout:** the hash stays in `progress.sent` (and in the browser, `pending.ts`), and the step shows **Waiting on Celo** (phase `uncertain`, error kind `uncertain`), with an explorer link.
  - **Nonce saved at send:** the wallet's pending nonce is read before the prompt and the transaction's own nonce after it (`getTransaction`), kept in `progress.nonces` and in the browser beside the hash.
  - **Checked before any new signature** (`src/lib/deploy/reconcile.ts`, `checkSentTx`): a receipt means wait and record it; known to the node means keep waiting; unknown with its nonce still free means dropped, so it is sent again **with that nonce**, so the two can't both be mined; unknown with its nonce used means the wallet replaced it (speed up or cancel), and for a creation the contract that nonce makes is looked up: if it exists, nothing is sent and the owner is told to find the transaction in their wallet's activity; if not, it is sent again. With no nonce saved it can't tell, and the owner decides. A network error while checking surfaces as an error and sends nothing.
  - **The modal:** Check again (signs nothing unless the chain showed a drop) and, behind a warning ("you'll have two contracts and pay the network fee twice"), Deploy again anyway, which forgets that hash here and in the browser.
  - Both paths now go through one `sendOnce`, so the upgradeable two-step deploy gets the same handling.
- **Checked:** `run.test.ts`: kept on a timeout and shown as waiting; a first transaction mined late is recorded with one signature (the reviewer's test); still pending sends nothing; dropped resends once with the saved nonce; replaced with a contract sends nothing and names it; replaced without one (a cancel) resends; no nonce leaves the choice to the owner; Deploy again anyway sends a second; the upgradeable code transaction is kept and checked. `reconcile.test.ts`: each outcome, the predicted contract address, and network errors surfacing. `pending.test.ts`: nonces saved and cleaned. `deploy-modal.test.tsx`: Waiting on Celo, Check again, and the warning. Lint, types, full suite.
- **UNVERIFIED:** whether MetaMask and other external wallets honour a dapp-set nonce (if one picks its own, the "nonce used" check still stops a duplicate on the next Check again, but not a second transaction already signed); the flow against a real slow Celo node.
- **Not done:** recording a contract that a wallet's sped-up transaction created. That needs the new transaction hash, which the owner finds in their wallet; a "record by transaction" form could come later.

### D109. Published apps with contracts always say "Unaudited contract"

- **Decided by:** the owner, 14 Nov 2026 ("fix the unaudited badge for visitors"), after GoodDollar's Daybreak Blue security review noted that a published, deployed app showed only the GoodBuild logo and an optional report link, never "Unaudited", and that the owner could hide the badge.
- **Now:** any published app whose project has its own contracts (`contracts/**/*.sol`, not tests) shows **Unaudited contract** in its bar (`UNAUDITED_LABEL`, `hasOwnContracts` in `src/server/publish/build.ts`). Like the demo and "can be changed" notices, it stays when the owner turns the badge off (D90's setting now removes only the logo). A visitor can still close the bar for their own visit. Apps without contracts are unchanged.
- **Existing apps:** a published page is a snapshot, so an app published before this shows the label from its next publish.
- **Checked:** `badge.test.ts` (shown with the badge off, logo still removed, tests not counted as contracts), `build.test.ts` (the built page carries it and keeps the space under the bar), the publish suite.

### D110. The mainnet gate records "passed" only for exploit tests that ran and passed, and money detection reads libraries, constructors and inherited code (GB-04, GB-05)

- **Decided by:** the owner agreed the scope (GB-04 and GB-05 only) in the brief for this change, 14 Nov 2026, after the security sweep (`docs/reviews/SWEEP_2026-11-14.md` on branch `claude/security-sweep-2026-11`). The call on case (f) below is Claude's, flagged for the owner to reconsider.
- **Found (reproduced, not only read):** the sweep ran nothing, so each claim was first written as a failing test and run on `main` with real `solc`. **Every one reproduced:**
  - *GB-04:* a `testExploitReentrancy` that exists only in a comment passed the gate; the same tests in `contracts/GoodPool.t.sol` (outside `contracts/test/`) passed without running, and `compileForDeploy` treated that file as a **deployable contract** (the test contract `GoodPoolTest` became an artifact); tests that only assert `assertTrue(true)` / `assertEq(1, 1)` passed; one name (`testExploitUnauthorizedReentrancyDonation`) met several needs; a test contract not named `*Test` (so never compiled as a suite) left zero executed tests and `exploitGate` still returned `passed`; a non-public test passed; `rolesOf` called a money-holding contract a vault from a comment or string containing `function deposit(` and `function withdraw(`, which skipped the owner's review.
  - *GB-05:* (a) a library function, a file-level function and a modifier that pull tokens; (b) a constructor that pulls tokens; (c) `ERC721Holder`, `ERC1155Holder`, `TimelockController` and `VestingWallet` bases (each one separately); (d) `ERC4626 as Base` and `Governor as Gov` aliases; (e) `Address.functionCall` and `functionCallWithValue`, through the `Address.` form and `using Address for address`; the ERC-1363 hooks; and (f) all were missed by `custodyOf` or the guardrails.
  - What held up and is kept (tested not to be newly flagged): a payable constructor, `super.transferFrom` in a token's own override, comments and strings, function names, plain ERC20/ERC721/ERC1155/AccessControl/Pausable/ReentrancyGuard, a contract that sends tokens but never reads its own balance, test-file helpers, and contracts that take in no money.
- **Now (GB-04):**
  - **"Passed" means ran and passed.** After the compile, the gate runs every test (as before), then requires each needed exploit test to be in the **executed results** with `ok`, matched to a test that is written as a public or external `testExploit...()` function that asserts something. No tests run with money-holding code present is a refusal ("No tests ran, so none of the exploit tests did"). `GateResult.exploitTests` is now `passed`, `not_required` or `failed`; a project with the kit on and nothing money-holding is `not_required`.
  - **Each need has its own test function** (a best assignment of tests to needs, so one name counts once however many patterns it matches).
  - **Comments and strings never count.** A small Solidity lexer (`src/server/agent/solidity-text.ts`, `stripSolidity`) blanks comments and string literals, keeping offsets; `rolesOf` and `exploitTests` read the blanked text. This also fixes `knownShape`: a contract that only mentions vault functions in a comment is no longer a "known shape" and needs the owner's review.
  - **A `*.t.sol` file anywhere is a test.** One rule (`src/lib/deploy/test-file.ts`: under `contracts/test/` or ending `.t.sol`) is used by the builder's checks, the deploy compile (`deployable`), contract state, the runner and the UI's "has contracts" tests. Files under `contracts/test/` that aren't `.t.sol` stay tests, so existing projects are unchanged.
  - **A test that asserts a constant doesn't count:** `assertTrue(true)`, `assertEq(1, 1)`, `assertGt(2, 1)`, `assertEq(x, x)`, or any assertion whose arguments are all literals. The builder's loop uses the same text rule (without the executed-results step, which only exists at the gate).
- **Now (GB-05):**
  - `custodyOf` scans libraries, file-level functions, constructors and modifiers for pulls, adds the ERC-1363 hooks (`onTransferReceived`, `onApprovalReceived`), and resolves bases through the compiler's **linearized base contracts**, so an import alias is seen and receivers inherited from OpenZeppelin (and anything else the compile imported) are found by reading the imported base, not by its written name. OpenZeppelin files are read, never reported. A contract that inherits something money-holding gets one line, "inherits ERC721Holder, which is called when tokens are sent to the contract" (ERC4626 and Governor keep their old wording). A pull anywhere in a project library or free function counts, even if no contract uses it yet: broad on purpose.
  - The guardrails (`guardrailIssues`, including `securityOnly`, so the deploy compile on every chain) now refuse `Address.functionCall` and `functionCallWithValue` (the `Address.` form, or called on an `address`), alongside `functionDelegateCall`.
  - **Case (f), decided: flagged, in a narrow form.** A contract that reads a token's balance of itself (`x.balanceOf(address(this))`) **and** sends tokens (`transfer` / `safeTransfer`) is treated as holding other people's money: anyone can fund it with a plain `transfer`, so its payout is someone else's money. The cost of a false positive is one owner confirmation. **Known limit:** a contract funded by plain `transfer` that pays out from its own bookkeeping and never reads `balanceOf(address(this))` is not seen, and a pull made through a contract the project doesn't contain can't be.
  - Custody stays a mainnet-gate rule only (D107): `checkContracts` still only reports it, and the builder loop requires nothing from it, so testnet builds are no slower and the demo tip jar and builder-loop tests are unchanged.
- **Who is affected (no migration):**
  - Contracts already deployed to mainnet are unaffected. Re-preparing a project for mainnet now refuses it if its exploit tests aren't in a real run, and now asks for the owner's review where it used to pass through as a "vault" by comment.
  - Newly refused at the deploy compile, on **every** chain (testnet too) and in the builder's check: a contract using `Address.functionCall` / `functionCallWithValue`.
  - Newly needing exploit tests and the owner's review at mainnet: pulls in libraries, free functions, constructors or modifiers; ERC-1363 receivers; bases such as `ERC721Holder`, `ERC1155Holder`, `TimelockController`, `VestingWallet`; contracts that read their own token balance and send tokens.
  - The shipped kits' contracts (game shop, game board, tip jar demo) are unaffected: the only `balanceOf(address(this))` in `src/` is inside a test.
  - `deploy_reviews` rows written before this change still say `passed` on the old meaning. `recordDeployment` accepts a row for the same source, and a new prepare overwrites it. Evidence item 6 in the sweep (count of mainnet rows and recorded values) is how to see how many there are; none can be told apart from the table alone.
- **Checked:** `gb04.test.ts` (the three sweep cases, one name for two needs, a non-public test, zero executed tests through `prepareDeploy` and through `exploitGate` with an empty run, a named test that never ran or failed, the comment-only deposit/withdraw contract still needing review, `stripSolidity`, `rolesOf` and `exploitTests` with comments and strings) and `gb05.test.ts` (one case per (a)-(f), ERC-1363, the held-up list) compile real Solidity through the same helpers the gate uses. `gb02.test.ts`, `dao-vault.test.ts` and `deploy.test.ts` pass unchanged. Lint, types, the full suite.
- **UNVERIFIED:** that the gate is sufficient. It shows the named tests ran and passed, **not that they are good**: a test can assert something real about a toy case and miss the actual flaw, and a constant-assertion check can't see `uint256 x = 1; assertEq(x, 1)`. The reviewer's other concern, that the gate is a quality check on AI-written code and not an anti-rug control (the owner and deployer are the same person), is unchanged. A real model's behaviour against the stricter rules, and the review screen's wording for the new "inherits ..." lines, in a real browser.
- **Not touched:** every other finding in the sweep (GB-06 to GB-20).

### D111. On-chain credit is keyed on the quote ref, waits 30 blocks, and the scan checkpoint trails head by 1,800 blocks (GB-13)

- **Decided by:** the owner agreed the scope (GB-13 only) in the brief for this change, 14 Nov 2026, after the security sweep (`docs/reviews/SWEEP_2026-11-14.md`). N, K and the "first copy of a ref wins" rule below are Claude's calls, flagged for the owner to reconsider. D110 was the highest number on `main` and on `claude/fix-gb04-gb05`.
- **Found (reproduced, not only read):** the sweep ran nothing, so each claim was first written as a failing test (`src/server/credits/gb13.test.ts`) and run on `main` at `8a2a7b0` on PGlite: 13 of its 14 tests failed. The one that passed is the check that old rows with no ref still credit, which is meant to pass on `main` too. **Every claim reproduced:**
  - *(a)* the same transaction and ref recorded at log index 0 and then 3 (a reorg copy) credited 1,000,000 instead of 500,000: two ledger entries.
  - *(b)* one quote ref paid by two transaction hashes (the first dropped) credited twice; four concurrent copies of one ref credited four times.
  - *(c)* `redeemFromReceipt` credited a receipt at depth 0, and the scan credited an event 2 blocks below head. `docs/CREDITS.md` said "wait N=3 blocks"; the code had no depth.
  - *(d)* with the log node 200 blocks behind the node that reported head (more than the 64-block overlap), the checkpoint moved to head and the payment was never credited after the node caught up.
  - *(e)* one log with `credits` = 2^80 made `recordRedemption` throw, so the scan threw, the checkpoint stayed put and the good payment after it in the same chunk was not credited. This would happen on every tick. It can't happen with the real contract's supply.
- **Now:**
  - **Credit once per (chain, ref).** `onchain_credit_events` has a nullable `ref` column and a partial unique index on (chain_id, ref) where ref is not null (migration 0021). The existing unique index on (chain, tx, log) is kept. New credits use the ledger key `onchain:<chainId>:<ref>`. The contract sets `used[ref]` before release, and `grant()` shares the map, so each ref is one purchase or grant. A ref seen again from another transaction or log index (a reorg copy, or a re-paid quote whose first transaction was dropped) is not stored or credited a second time. It is logged (`[credits] … seen again …`), with a second line if the wallet or amount differ. **The first row recorded for a ref is the one kept**, so after a dropped-then-re-paid quote the row names the dropped transaction hash, and the log line names both. Every chain-sourced event must carry a 32-byte ref (`recordRedemption` refuses one without).
  - **Old rows keep working.** Rows recorded before 0021 have no ref and keep their old key (chain + tx + log index), so the 9 production events stay credited exactly once. When such a row is read again at its exact log, its ref is filled in; it is not re-credited, because it already has a ledger entry. The migration does not backfill from the network.
  - **Confirmation depth N = 30 blocks.** The scan reads only up to head − 30. `redeemFromReceipt` answers `pending` until head − receipt block ≥ 30, so the route returns 202 and the browser's existing polling (40 tries, 3 s apart, at least 120 s) waits. If a lagging node reports a head below the receipt, that reads as "not deep enough", which only waits.
  - **Checkpoint margin K = 1,800 blocks.** The checkpoint never records a block newer than head − 1,800. Each scan resumes at `min(checkpoint + 1, head − 1,800 + 1)`, so it always re-reads the newest 1,800 blocks, even if the checkpoint was put inside that window by older code or by a server whose node reported a later head. This **supersedes D106's 64-block re-read**. The GB-01 guarantees are kept and still tested: an empty gap over 50,000 blocks is crossed, an older unreported payment is still credited after a newer direct receipt, a failure part-way leaves the checkpoint at the last full chunk, and it never moves backward (`greatest`).
  - **A malformed log is skipped, not fatal (e).** A log whose amount isn't a positive whole number that can be counted exactly, or that has no valid ref, is logged with `console.error` (`[credits] skipped a malformed Redeemed log …`) and skipped, and the checkpoint moves on. Database and RPC errors still throw and leave the checkpoint where it was, as before.
- **Why N = 30 and K = 1,800 (measured on forno.celo.org, 14 Nov 2026, read-only `eth_getBlockByNumber`, three samples):**
  - Celo makes one block per second: 224 blocks in 224 s, and 21 in 21 s.
  - forno supports both tags. `safe` trailed `latest` by 224, 339 and 89 blocks (1.5 to 5.7 min); `finalized` trailed by 1,491, 1,220 and 1,241 (20 to 25 min). Both are too slow for the browser's two-minute wait, and waiting on `safe` would leave most buyers on "paid, credit on its way".
  - So N is a fixed depth that fits the wait with room to spare: 30 s, a quarter of the window. It is past the short reorgs of the newest blocks, but it is **not** finality.
  - K is a bound on how far a log node may lag without the scan skipping anything: 30 minutes, which also covers the measured distance to `finalized`, so a reorg short of finality is re-read. It costs one extra `eth_getLogs` over at most 1,800 blocks per scan, plus re-reading the events in that window (cheap at current volume).
- **Who is affected:**
  - A buyer's credit now lands about 30 seconds after the payment is mined instead of at once. The dialog shows its existing "crediting" state meanwhile. A reverted or non-credits transaction also gets its "didn't go through" answer about 30 s later.
  - **Missing a payment?** answers "isn't confirmed yet" for a transaction under 30 blocks deep.
  - Owner `grant()`s wait the same 30 blocks. If Celo stalls for over two minutes the dialog shows "paid, credit on its way" (as before), and the scan credits it later.
- **Migration 0021 is additive:** `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN "ref" text` (nullable, no default) and `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX … ("chain_id","ref") WHERE ref is not null`. Every existing row has a null ref, so the index can't conflict. Code from before this change ignores the column and keeps inserting rows with a null ref, so a rollback of the code is safe with the migration in place. While old code runs, only the tx + log protection applies, as before.
- **Checked:**
  - `gb13.test.ts` (14 tests: (a), (b) ×3 including concurrent copies and a mismatched amount, (c) receipt and scan, (d) lagging node and the head − K bound, (e), the migration's old-row, fill-in, ref-key and unique-index cases, and a ref being required).
  - `onchain.test.ts`, which keeps all the GB-01 cases. Three expected values changed by design: the checkpoint is now head − K where it was head, and the last re-read range is the newest K blocks where it was 64. The fixtures now give each event its own ref and a receipt deep enough to credit.
  - `purchase-flow.test.ts` (real contract on a local EVM; its receipt shim now reports a head 30 blocks past the receipt), `credits-contract.test.ts` and `quote.test.ts`.
  - All credits tests on PGlite and on real Postgres 16 (`TEST_DATABASE_URL`, local). Lint, types and the full suite.
  - `scripts/credits-reconcile.ts` run against a local Postgres seeded with old-style rows, ref rows and a planted duplicate.
- **UNVERIFIED:**
  - How often Celo reorgs past 30 blocks. No reorg history was measured. If one drops a credited payment and the buyer never re-pays, the credit stands with no G$ moved; that window is now 30 blocks where it was 0.
  - That the `safe` and `finalized` distances measured in three samples are typical.
  - A **pre-0021 row** is protected only at its exact log: a reorg copy of one of those transactions at another log index would be credited again. The 9 production rows are hundreds of thousands of blocks deep, far past finality, so this can't happen to them.
  - The new 30-second wait in a real browser and on a phone. The production reconcile after deploy (owner to run: expect 9 events, 0 duplicate ledger entries, 0 refs with more than one entry; refs fill in only if an old transaction is read again).
- **Not touched:** the contract, the quote flow, and every other finding in the sweep (GB-14 included, which the sweep's fix plan groups with GB-13).

### D112. Games are judged by their own kind: action, puzzle or turns, played with their own controls (step 1 of GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2)

- **Decided by:** the owner chose level L2 for game templates and said "Yes, start step 1 and continue autonomously" (17 Nov 2026). The design below is Claude's, from [GAMES_PLAN.md](GAMES_PLAN.md) Part 2, phase H.
- **The problem.** The game check (D94) judged every game as an arcade game: an obstacle within 3.5 seconds, a speed that rises, a player whose position moves. A quiz, a match puzzle or a card game got false issues, and each one sends the fix loop to bolt things to dodge onto a puzzle. The bot only tapped the sides and pressed keys, so a drag or swipe game was reported as "controls do nothing". A game with no `GameCanvas` (a quiz of buttons) had no way to report its rounds, so pressing Play was reported as starting nothing.
- **What changed:**
  - `declareGame` takes `style` (`"action"`, `"puzzle"` or `"turns"`) and `controls` (`"tap"`, `"sides"`, `"keys"`, `"swipe"`, `"drag"`, `"hold"`, `"buttons"`). A game that says neither is an action game, judged exactly as before.
  - New probe calls: `moved()` for every move a puzzle or turns game accepts, and `round(true)` / `round(false)` for a game with no `GameCanvas` (it keeps its own clock). `probe(score)` takes the position and speed as optional.
  - **Rules by kind.** Action: unchanged. Puzzle and turns: no obstacle, speed or movement rules; instead "never accepted a move" (after 4 seconds of play) and "score never went up" (only after 16 moves, so a quiz answered at random almost never trips it: 0.75^16 is about 1%). Declared kinds and Play again are judged for every kind.
  - **The bot plays the declared controls**: swipes, drags, holds, and presses the game's own buttons (inside `<div data-game-area>` when the game marks one, never wallet, share, mute or post buttons). Taps still go through the game area itself, so a panel over the game is never pressed by accident; a drag or hold first checks that the point is on the game.
  - **Spawns on a timer.** `GameCanvas` marks its frames (`checkFrame`), so the check sees a declared obstacle made outside a frame (by `setInterval` or `setTimeout`) and reports it in an action game. That is a real player bug, not only a check problem: a timer keeps spawning while the game is paused or over. Chosen over a file check, which can't tell a spawn timer from `setTimeout(() => setToast(null))` and would cause false fix loops. A project whose `GameCanvas` is changed or older never marks frames, so it is never judged on this.
  - **File checks.** A game with no `GameCanvas` (and no three.js) is asked for `declareGame`, `round` and `probe`, but only once it looks like a game (it calls the probe or shows a Play or Start button). A puzzle or turns game is asked for `moved()`.
  - The run check only calls a Play that starts nothing a fault when the game can report rounds: a `GameCanvas` that knows how, or `round()` in the game's own code. This should stop a 3D scene that borrows the 2D kit's sound getting a false "Play started nothing" (the bug suspected in GAMES_PLAN.md section 7, row 2): **UNVERIFIED**, no 3D run-check test shows it yet (step 2).
  - **What changes for games already built.** Their play is judged as before. Two things differ: the bot's default moves are now half taps on the sides and half keys (Up and Down added), where they were two taps to three keys; and a game with no `GameCanvas` that shows a Play button but no probe calls now gets the file check above the next time it is edited, as D94 did for `GameCanvas` games. Kept on purpose: such a game can't be tested otherwise.
  - `GameCanvas.tsx` and `probe.ts` are new kit versions; today's are in `superseded`, so untouched copies in projects already built get the new ones.
- **Checked:** `game-check.test.ts` (each kind's rules, rounds with moves and no frames, the timer rule only with marked frames and only for action games, old games still judged as action). In a real headless browser (`kits/game-2d.test.ts`): a quiz of buttons with no canvas passes with no issues as a turns game; the same quiz with dead answers is caught; a drag-steered game passes, and the same game without `controls: ["drag"]` is reported as "never moved the player" (run once by hand as a control, to show the test depends on the new bot); a game that spawns with `setInterval` is caught. All earlier game tests pass. Lint and types clean on the changed files.
- **Not caused by this change:** four `kits/three-d.test.ts` tests failed because the local `publish-runtime.pack.gz` (git-ignored, built 5 Nov) predates three.js, and the publish build prefers it over `publish-runtime/`. Rebuilt with `node scripts/build-publish-runtime.mjs`; they pass. Anyone with an old pack will see the same. One `wallet-layer.test.ts` test ("expected Close to contain MetaMask") fails the same way on `main` at c5a7b7cd1; not a games matter, left alone.
- **UNVERIFIED:** a real model writing `style`, `controls`, `moved()` and `round()` from the guide; the planner still asks every game about obstacles, power-ups and characters (phase H5, step 5 of the plan).

### D113. 3D games are tested like 2D games, and come back from a lost 3D context (step 2 of GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2)

- **Decided by:** the owner (L2, "continue autonomously", 17 Nov 2026). Design by Claude, from GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2, phase I.
- **The problem.** A 3D game got one check: is the scene a flat colour. Which kits it got depended on its words: "make a 3D racing game" turned on the 2D games kit too, "a 3D maze game" and "a 3D flying game" didn't, so those had no probe, building blocks, leaderboard or shop. `Scene3D` never reported rounds or ran at check speed, and the run check keyed the full play test on the 2D kit being on, so a 3D game with that kit was judged by 2D start-screen rules against a WebGL canvas. A cheap phone that drops the 3D context left a black box until a reload.
- **What changed:**
  - **Every 3D game gets the games kit** (runner: the 3D kit is on and the request is a game by `isGameRequest`). A 3D viewer doesn't.
  - **`Scene3D` reports to the check itself**, reading `window.__gbGame` directly so a 3D project needs nothing from `src/game/`: a round for each stretch of unpaused play, up to 8 steps per frame at check speed, frames marked (so spawns on a timer are caught as in 2D), and `data-game-canvas` on its canvas. New `onIdleFrame` for the start screen (the scene is already drawn while paused).
  - **Lost context:** `Scene3D` keeps the context restorable, shows "The game paused to save memory. Tap to reload it." over the scene, and builds the scene again on a tap or when the browser gives the context back.
  - **`Hud3D`** (`src/three/Hud3D.tsx`): the score panel over a 3D scene, reading the game each frame and writing to the page without re-rendering React.
  - **The run check** treats a project that uses `<Scene3D` with `declareGame` as a 3D game: probe on, start screen judged by the 3D canvas (read from inside its frame, twice), Play found, the bot plays, Play again, wallet, leaderboard and MiniPay as for 2D. Messages say Scene3D, the frame function, and x and z. A project that uses `<Scene3D` is never judged by the 2D start-screen rules.
  - **Guides:** the 3D guide says which parts of the games kit apply (probe, blocks on x and z, sound, leaderboard, GameOver, shop) and which don't (GameCanvas, drawHud, Sprite, particles); the 2D guide points 3D games to Scene3D. A file check asks a 3D game for `declareGame`, `spawned` and `probe`.
  - The game checks now ignore every kit's files when reading the builder's code (a dropdown's `align="start"` was read as a Start button). The "has a Play button" test only matches a capitalised Play or Start as button text or a string.
  - Today's `Scene3D` is in `superseded`.
- **Checked** (real headless Chromium with WebGL): a 3D lane-dodge game written the way the guide asks passes the app and type checks and is played for real (3 rounds, 25 s of game, its blocks seen, no issues); the same game with steering removed is caught in 3D words; one with no probe calls is caught by the file check; the review's start and playing pictures of a 3D game come out drawn (Playwright's screenshot, each picture over 8 KB); losing the context with `WEBGL_lose_context` shows the tap-to-reload button and a tap brings back a live context. The runner turns the games kit on for "A 3D maze game where a monster chases you" and not for "A spinning 3D viewer for my NFT art". Full agent suite (444 tests), lint and types clean.
- **UNVERIFIED:** whether the production checker's browser has WebGL. Without it, `Scene3D` shows its plain message and reports no rounds, and the run check leaves a 3D game unplayed with the note "no WebGL on this checker" instead of a false fix request (fixed after review; tested with WebGL turned off). Also UNVERIFIED: the workspace's own in-browser picture (html-to-image, used by the visual check) of a WebGL canvas, which may still come out blank because `Scene3D` doesn't keep its drawing buffer; a real phone losing its context; a real model following the new 3D game guide. Not built here: a 3D `Sprite` for store cards (plan I3), left for the 3D templates (step 4), which will show whether it's needed.

### D114. Game templates: a working, tested game the builder reshapes, picked by the planner (step 3 of GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2)

- **Decided by:** the owner (L2, "continue autonomously", 17 Nov 2026). Design by Claude, from GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2, phases J and K. This step is the first slice the advisor asked for: the mechanism plus two templates that exercise both kinds of check (an action game with a canvas, a turns game without one). The other eight come after.
- **What changed:**
  - **`src/server/agent/game-templates/`**: one module per template (id, plain name, 2D or 3D, style, which requests it fits, the kits it needs, its files, whether it has the leaderboard, and what the builder is told). `withGameTemplate` turns its kits on, writes its files over the starter's and adds the scores rule to `dataRules.json` without touching other rules.
  - **Lane racer**: three lanes; traffic that blinks before changing lane, oncoming cars, a rival who blocks, oil that takes the wheel for a moment, nitro and a shield; near-miss combos; the score panel, particles, shake and popups; a first-run tip; pause on a hidden tab; GameOver on the game with PostScore; the top three over the road and the full board below; settings. Every kind arrives early, in order, so a short round meets them all. Pools and Spawners throughout: nothing is made while it runs.
  - **Quick quiz**: no canvas. Today's set in the same order for everyone (`dailySeed`), 12 seconds a question with a bar, three lives, three right in a row doubles the points, a hint that takes away two wrong answers (another every three in a row), GameOver with PostScore, the board. Reports `round()`, `moved()` and `probe(score)`, with the answers in `data-game-area`.
  - **The planner picks one.** `game_brief.template` is one of the ids or `"none"`, with each template's one-line fit in the field's description. An id GoodBuild doesn't have is dropped. The plan card says "Starts from a ready-made lane racer we reshape for you." Nobody is asked to choose and no technical word is shown.
  - **The runner** adds the template on the first build only, after the kits, and gives the builder the template's brief: what each file is, what to rename and restyle, what to change to fit the plan, and what to keep (probe calls, pools, GameOver, free play). The files are the person's from then on: no `superseded`, never put back.
  - **The board test in the run check** now gets up to three rounds to score something, because a random player can lose a quiz round with nothing scored, which left the board untested.
- **Why not a kit per template, or prose only:** kits never come off and their files come back when deleted, which is wrong for a game the person reshapes. Prose (like page templates) leaves the model to write the game again, which is what caused the retries.
- **Checked:** both templates pass the app check and the type check as shipped, and in a real headless browser the run check plays each with no issues and posts a score that shows on the board. Lane racer's note lists all six kinds; a copy with steering removed is caught; a copy that never spawns oil is either caught or reported as not judged, never passed. The quiz is judged as a turns game by its answers; a copy with dead answers is caught. A first build from a plan that picked Lane racer has its files, the games kit, the scores rule, the log line and the brief in the builder's context; a made-up template id is dropped; the plan card line is plain words. Full suite: 1,581 pass; the two failures (`public-stats` week counts, the wallet list) fail the same way without this change.
- **Fixed after review (same day):**
  - The template's kits were added after the runner decided on the board and shop kits, which ask whether the games kit will be on. "A racing game for MiniPay ... with a leaderboard" from Lane racer got no board contract (the test failed first, then passed). The template's kits now go in first.
  - On MiniPay, a template's `src/App.tsx` replaced the starter's and so had no info pages or footer, and its board didn't post to the contract: four app-check errors on every MiniPay template build, each a fix-loop step. `withGameTemplate` now writes a MiniPay app (the game on the home route, the five info pages, MiniFooter) when the MiniPay kit is on, and passes the board contract to `useLeaderboard` when its kit is on. Both templates with the MiniPay and board kits pass the app check (all but MINI_APP's wording, which the builder fills in and is told to) and the type check against the compiled contract.
  - The quiz's clock re-rendered React every frame, which the game guide forbids and builders copy. It now lives in a ref and draws its bar straight to the page. Right and wrong answers use the theme's `bg-success` and `bg-danger`, not fixed colours.
- **UNVERIFIED:** a real model picking the right template and reshaping it well (the evals need the owner's keys, D70); how a template looks in a real build next to DESIGN_SYSTEM.md (they use the generated app's theme tokens: `bg-card`, `bg-primary`, `bg-success`, `bg-danger`, `text-muted-foreground`); a MiniPay template build in the real MiniPay app.

### D115. The planner asks each kind of game its own questions, and every game is original (step 5 of GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2, phases H5 and H6)

- **Decided by:** the owner (L2, "continue autonomously", 17 Nov 2026). Design by Claude.
- **The problem.** The Quick quiz template (D114) could be picked, yet a quiz's plan still asked "What should get in the player's way?", "Which power-ups?" and "Who else is in the game, controlled by the computer?": the model was told to always ask those three, and the server added generic cards for any it left out. Requests that name an existing game ("make Tetris") were built as asked: nothing told the planner or builder otherwise (GAMES_PLAN.md section 7, row 5).
- **What changed:**
  - `game_brief.style` (`action`, `puzzle`, `turns`). The kind is the template's when one is picked, else the model's, else from the request's words (`styleFromWords`: quiz, trivia, card or board games are `turns`; puzzle, match-3, sudoku, word or memory games are `puzzle`; anything else `action`).
  - For a puzzle, quiz or turn-based game the three cards are "What should make it hard?" (a clock, a limit on moves or lives, harder rounds, choices that look alike), "What should help the player?" (hints, undo or skip, extra time, streak bonus) and "Who do people play against?" (their own best, a computer player, everyone's scores, a friend taking turns). The model is told the same, and the server's stand-in cards and the fallback plan follow the kind. The card matcher also recognises "makes it hard", "helps the player", "hints" and "play against".
  - The build is told the kind of game, with the `declareGame` style to use.
  - **Original games only**, in three places: the planner (keep the kind of play, a new name, its own look and characters, and say so in the pitch), the builder's system prompt (any named game, app, brand or character), and the top of the games guide. The game words still match "tetris" and so on, so the games kit still turns on.
  - The run check's board test now gets up to six rounds within 12 seconds to score something, not three: a quiz answered at random scores nothing in about 4 rounds in 10, so three rounds still missed about 7% of the time (seen once in the full suite).
- **Checked:** `plan-first.test.ts`: the kind from words (12 requests); a quiz's fallback and parsed plans have the three calm cards and none of the action ones, and the build is told "Kind of game: turns"; a racer still gets the action cards; a template's kind wins over the model's; the originality rule is in the planner's rules, the context for "make tetris for my class", the builder's prompt and the guide. The planning headroom test still passes. Game, 3D and template tests twice in a row, all passing; full suite as before (the stats and wallet-list failures are the same without this change).
- **UNVERIFIED:** whether a real model follows the originality rule every time. There is no server check that a game's name isn't an existing game's; that would need a list of names and would still miss characters and art.

### D116. The first 3D game template: 3D runner (step 4 of GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2, first of eight)

- **Decided by:** the owner (L2, "continue autonomously", 17 Nov 2026). Built first among the remaining eight, on the advisor's advice, because no 3D template existed and 3D play had never run together with posting to the board. The other seven wait for the owner to look at the first three.
- **What it is:** run down a three-lane track; tap a side or press left or right to change lane, the action button or Space to jump. Crates to dodge, low red barriers to jump, a drone that sweeps across the lanes, coins and a shield. Every kind arrives early, in order. Built from three.js shapes made once and reused; the games kit's Spawner, difficulty, Effects, sound, haptics and leaderboard; Hud3D for the score, coins and shield timer; GameOver with PostScore; TopThree over the scene; a drifting camera on the start screen through `onIdleFrame`. Needs the 3D and games kits, which the template turns on. With the MiniPay kit it gets the same pages, footer and board wiring as the 2D templates.
- **Checked** (headless Chromium with WebGL): app check and type check as shipped, and with the MiniPay and board kits against the compiled contract; the run check plays it with no issues, sees all five kinds and posts a score that shows on the board; a copy with steering removed is caught in 3D words. The check's own start and playing pictures show the scene drawn.
- **Also after review:** Lane racer's car drawing guards `ctx.roundRect`, which older phones (iOS 15 Safari) lack; the kit's own score panel (`drawPanel` in `hud.ts`, on main since D94) still calls it unguarded, so a 2D game crashes on its first frame there. Left for a separate kit fix (it needs a `superseded` entry for `hud.ts`). With the MiniPay kit on, each template's run check takes about 18 to 22 seconds here (limit 50). A quiz gets no "playing" picture for the review, because that picture is taken only when a game canvas is visible.
- **UNVERIFIED:** how it runs on a cheap phone (it has about 30 meshes and two lights, no shadows); a real model reshaping it.

### D117. The other seven game templates, and calm games get longer to end a round (step 4 of GAMES_PLAN.md Part 2, finished)

- **Decided by:** the owner ("Build the other 7", 17 Nov 2026). Design by Claude.
- **What changed:**
