  - **Four 2D templates.** **Catch and dodge:** move a basket by dragging, tapping a side or the arrows; catch fruit, dodge rocks and the eggs a passing bird drops; a magnet and a slow-down; three lives and combos. **Cave flyer:** hold to climb through a winding cave; walls push the craft back rather than end the run, pillars always leave a gap of at least a third; gems, a shield, and a bat that turns toward the player after a warning. **Brick breaker:** paddle, up to three balls that serve themselves, a 7 by 5 wall with hard bricks, a drifter that drops bombs, wide-paddle and extra-ball power-ups, a bonus for each cleared wall. **Match grid:** a 7 by 8 grid of buttons in four colours (no canvas); tap a group of two or more to clear it, a group of five or more leaves a bomb that clears 3 by 3, stones crack when a neighbour clears; 20 moves; today's board is the same for everyone.
  - **Three 3D templates.** **Kart track:** an endless winding road; steer by tapping where to go or with the arrows; off the road is slow, the tyre walls cost a life; cones, barrels, coins, boost pads and a slower rival worth points when passed. **Sky rings:** fly a plane through rings, steering toward where the player taps or with the arrows; rings score and refill the fuel, which runs down all the time; rocks, a hawk that crosses from the side, a boost that doubles points. **Maze escape:** a 13 by 13 maze, new each level, built from today's seed; one step per swipe, tap or arrow; find the key, then the door; gems, spikes that rise on a beat, guards on the longest corridors (one more each maze); a torch of 20 seconds that gems and doors top up.
  - **Shared:** `shared.ts` holds the plain `App.tsx` and the brief's standard closing lines. All ten templates are in `GAME_TEMPLATES`, so the planner offers them all.
  - **Calm games get longer to end a round** in the run check's board test: 10 seconds a round and 16 in all for puzzle and turns games, instead of 6 and 12 for action games. A puzzle played by random taps ends a 20-move round more slowly than an action game ends by crashing.
- **Where the build differs from GAMES_PLAN.md J3 and J4** (each change keeps the template playable by the check's bot, which plays at random for about 28 seconds of game time):
  - Match grid clears tapped groups instead of swapping to match three, and has no goals per level: swaps by random swipes almost never matched, so no round ended.
  - Kart track is an endless road, not laps, and stays upright (no "Turn your phone"). Sky rings has a fuel gauge instead of wind and towers, because a bot that never crashed never posted a score. Maze escape has patrolling guards and a torch instead of a chaser and a minimap.
  - Brick breaker doesn't save a level mid-run.
- **Checked** (headless Chromium with WebGL): every template passes the app check and the type check as shipped and with the MiniPay and board kits. Each was played by the run check 3 to 5 times in a row with no issues and a score posted and shown on the board. Broken copies are caught: a Match grid whose tiles do nothing ("never accepted a move"), a Catch and dodge whose basket never follows, a Brick breaker whose paddle never follows, a Sky rings whose steering does nothing, and a Maze escape that never takes a step ("never moved the player"). The names test bans breakout, arkanoid, bejeweled, mario kart and helicopter game as well. Full suite: 1,619 pass; the two failures (`public-stats` week counts, the wallet list) are the same as before (D114).
- **A gap in the check, so GAMES_PLAN's bar ("a broken copy of each template must fail") is not met for two templates:** a Kart track with dead steering and a Cave flyer whose hold does nothing both pass the check with no issues. The bends push the kart off the tyre walls and gravity pulls the craft down, so the player moves anyway, and the check only asks whether the player moved, not whether the controls moved them. If a builder breaks the controls while reshaping either, the person gets a game they can't steer and the check says it's fine. D118 now catches the Cave flyer case; Kart track is still open (Q46). Brick breaker's broken copy (a paddle that never follows) is caught. Maze escape now puts the player straight back at the start (instead of gliding) after a new maze or a hit, so that only the player's own steps move them; after that change it was played 6 times in a row with a score posted each time.
- **The owner kept the differences from J3 and J4 above** ("Keep them", 17 Nov 2026). That also answers GAMES_PLAN's open questions 2 (the template list as built) and 3 (sideways games: all upright for now).
- **UNVERIFIED:** how any of these feel on a real phone, and whether the 3D ones run smoothly on a cheap one; whether the 20-second torch and 20 moves are fun rather than just short enough for the check; a real model reshaping each; whether the production checker has WebGL (D116). Not built: `<Sprite>` drawings reused in the store for these templates.

### D118. The check sees whether the controls move the player in games that move the player by themselves; the score panel works on older phones; a slow-phone run

- **Decided by:** the owner ("continue with the unfinished work", 17 Nov 2026, after PR #112 listed these as open). Design by Claude, with the advisor.
- **Controls that do nothing (Q46, partly built).** "The player moved" proves nothing in a game that moves the player by itself. A Cave flyer whose hold did nothing passed, because gravity moved the craft.
  - **How it checks:** after the rounds, the bot leaves the player alone for a second. If the game moves them on its own, the bot holds one input and then the opposite, in turn (ABBA, so the game's own pull cancels out), six times. The inputs are a press against no press for a game that declares hold, and left against right for keys. If the movement doesn't follow the input consistently (a t statistic under 3), it reports that the controls do nothing.
  - **What it leaves alone:** games that don't move the player by themselves, games that declare only taps, swipes or buttons, and anything that isn't an action game. When rounds end too fast to judge, it says so in the note and is never held against the game.
  - **The probe change:** it now records where the player is (`at`). The old `probe.ts` is superseded, so untouched copies update. A project whose probe doesn't say where the player is (built before this change, or changed) isn't judged; tested.
  - **Calibrated on** all ten templates, 5 runs each, twice: no working template was ever flagged (100 runs). A dead Cave flyer was caught 5 times in 5. Dead copies scored at most 1.6 and working ones at least 6.0, against the cut-off of 3. The slowest check went from about 22s to about 23s (limit 50). Under the full suite's load the dead Cave flyer was once left unjudged (rounds ended before a clean stretch); the check then says "the controls weren't judged" in its note, and the test accepts that or the catch, never silence.
  - **Not caught: Kart track.** Without input, its kart moves only when it hits a tyre wall, which happens too rarely in a second for the check to see. A dead Kart track was caught 1 time in 5, so it has no test, and a builder who breaks its steering still gets a pass. Q46 stays open for that case. Judging every game that declares keys would catch it, but would risk telling people their controls are broken when they aren't (a maze player in a corridor can't step left or right). A wrong flag sends the fix loop after a bug that isn't there.
- **The score panel on older phones.** `drawPanel` in the games kit's `hud.ts` called `ctx.roundRect` unguarded, so on a browser without it (iOS 15 Safari) every 2D game crashed on its first frame. It now draws the same corners with `arcTo` there, and the old `hud.ts` is superseded. The test runs Catch and dodge with `roundRect` deleted from the browser: it crashed before the fix ("ctx.roundRect is not a function") and plays cleanly after.
- **A slow-phone run.** The run check takes test-only options: a script to run before the app, a stand-in phone (touch screen, CPU slowed 4 times through Chrome's own throttling), and a hook after play. All ten templates played this way with no issues, posted a score and held 60 frames a second (16.7ms median, 16.8ms at the 95th percentile, over 2.5 seconds of play at normal speed). Catch and dodge keeps this as a test. The page was already checked at 375 by 812, so Play again on screen and the layout were covered before.
- **UNVERIFIED:**
  - A real phone. Headless Chrome draws 3D in software, and slowing the CPU doesn't slow a graphics chip, so the 3D frame times say nothing about a cheap phone's GPU.
  - A real model picking and reshaping each template (the evals need the owner's provider keys, D70).
  - Whether the production checker has WebGL (D116).

### D119. Fixes from the PR #112 code review

- **Decided by:** the owner turned on Auto-fix for PR #112 (17 Nov 2026); the fixes answer CodeRabbit's review. Design by Claude.
- **The 3D kit (`Scene3D`, superseded so untouched copies update):**
  - It now holds the game still while the phone has taken the drawing context away. Before, the game kept playing behind the "Tap to reload" message, so a player could be hit or scored unseen. Tested by counting frames while the context is lost: 31 frames ran before the fix and none after.
  - A drag is applied once per screen frame. It used to be applied once per step when the check speeds a game up, which multiplied it.
  - `pointer.pressed` is true for one frame when a press starts. Taps too quick for a frame to see them held are no longer missed.
- **Templates:**
  - Lane runner (3D) steers when a press starts. Before, it steered when the pointer moved, so two taps on the same side counted once.
  - Catch and dodge, Brick breaker, Kart track and Sky rings move at a steady speed with the keys, whatever the frame rate.
  - Lane racer no longer gives a near-miss for a car that a shield or boost has destroyed.
  - In Quick quiz, a hint can't be taken twice on the same question.
  - Maze escape keeps its maze, key, gems, spikes and guards when the scene is rebuilt after a lost context. Before, it showed an empty maze. Not run on a phone; the draw step is shared with a new maze, which the run check plays.
- **The run check:**
  - It presses buttons inside the game area even when their names look like menu buttons.
  - Drags and swipes now go up and down as well as sideways.
  - A 3D game counts as having a blank start only when it has a scene.
  - The "playing" screenshot is taken for games drawn without a canvas too.
  - The 3D game is skipped as "no WebGL" only when the browser really has none. With WebGL, a game that never shows its scene is played and reported. Tested: before the fix, such a game was waved through as "no WebGL".
  - The controls check (D118) tests the arrow keys only in a game that also declares sideways steering ("sides" or "drag"). "keys" alone may mean only Space, as in a runner that jumps, so arrows that do nothing prove nothing there. A game that declares no controls isn't judged.
  - When posting a score to the board, a round that is still running can use the whole time the check allows (12 seconds, or 16 for calm games). Before, each round was stopped after 6 seconds (10 for calm games). On GitHub's slower test machine, Brick breaker and the 3D runner then reported "no round ended" and posted nothing.
  - **The controls check now reports only a hold that does nothing** (Cave flyer). For steering by keys or touch, it tries the arrows, then a finger pressed in the middle and dragged to each side over several frames. If either moves the player, the controls pass. If neither does, the check says in its note that the controls weren't judged, and doesn't report them.
    - **Why:** "keys" may mean only Space. A game that steers by how far the finger moved since the last frame sees little of a drag on a busy machine. Run alongside the 3D tests, a windy Catch and dodge with working drag steering was wrongly flagged. A wrong report sends the fix loop after a bug that isn't there, and the keys path had never been shown to catch anything (Kart track: 1 in 5, D118).
    - **Tested:** a windy Catch and dodge steered only by touch passes, as does one steered only by dragging. One with neither is left unjudged; before this change it was flagged.
    - **Too slow to judge:** the controls are also left unjudged when the game runs at less than half the check's speed against the clock. This can only turn a verdict into "not judged", never into a report, and has no test of its own.
- **Second round of review (3D kit):**
  - A new scene clears the lost-context flag. Before, a person who tapped reload before the browser gave the old context back got a frozen game. Tested: frames resume after a manual reload; they stayed at 5 before the fix.
  - When the browser restores the context by itself, the flag also stays set until the new scene is built, so the old scene never takes another step. Tested: frames stop while the context is lost and resume after the browser restores it.
  - A tap or drag on the start or game-over screen isn't counted as steering, and pausing clears any queued presses and drag. Before, a tap or drag made before Play moved the player on the first frame of play. Tested: both are dropped, and a tap during play still counts.
  - The check's bot holds a drag down for a frame before moving it, so a game that reads `pointer.down` each frame sees the press.
  - `pointer.presses` lists every press since the last frame, with where it started. Two quick taps count as two, and a later move doesn't change their side. The 3D runner steers on each press. Tested with two left taps and a move to the right, all in one frame.
  - The slow-phone test keeps a sample only if the same round ran from its start to its end.
- **The games kit:** a canvas puzzle or turns game isn't asked to call `spawned()`, since it may have nothing that appears. Action games still are. Tested both ways.
- **Tests made stricter:**
  - The slow-phone test measures frames only during a live round, starting one with Play again if needed.
  - The test for an older probe must have actually run.
- **Checked:** the kit, template and games-kit tests pass (92). The full suite gives the same two failures as on main (D114).
- **Still open:** Kart track with dead steering (Q46, D118).

### D120. Paying or deploying reconnects the signed-in wallet instead of asking to connect again

- **Date:** 18 Nov 2026 · **Decided by:** the owner reported it ("when my user tried to buy credit, it says they should connect wallet again to pay") and approved the fix. Design by Claude, reviewed by the advisor.
- **The bug:** the GoodBuild session (7 days, ours) and the browser's wallet link are separate. After a reload the page quietly tries to get the wallet back once, at load. It missed when the wallet extension announced itself a moment late, was locked, or the email wallet's Magic login had ended. The top bar still showed the account (from the session), the Pay button was on, and the payment then failed with "Connect a wallet first." Deploys, the admin panel and moving old funds had the same gap, and the deploy button stayed greyed out.
- **The fix (one place, `src/lib/auth/magic/magic-auth.tsx`):**
  - New `ensureWallet()` on the auth API. `getWalletClient()` and `switchChain()` use it, so every caller gets it. When signed in with no live wallet, it reconnects the wallet the session was signed with: the remembered wallet (or the one wallet in the browser if none was remembered), asked with `eth_requestAccounts` so a locked wallet opens; WalletConnect reopens its QR.
  - It never uses a different account: the server signed the credit quote for the session's address. It says "Your wallet has a different account selected. Switch to 0x12…34, the one you signed in with, then try again." This also covers a wallet that switched accounts after the page loaded. For an email account the message says another email wallet is signed in here and to Disconnect and sign in with the email again, since an email wallet has no accounts to switch.
  - WalletConnect's client is made once per page and reused (making it again on every check upsets its relay); the quiet restore tries WalletConnect and email wallets once, and browser wallets again on each late announcement.
  - An email wallet whose Magic login ended can't be reopened without a code, so it says to choose Disconnect and sign in with the email again, and that the account and apps stay the same.
  - The quiet restore at load runs again when a wallet announces itself late.
  - Disconnect now logs out of Magic for an email account even when the page never got the email wallet back.
  - The deploy button, the faucet guide and the "send CELO" box use the session's address while the wallet reconnects.
- **Tests:** nine in `magic-auth.test.tsx` (late wallet, locked wallet on Pay, no remembered wallet, a different account refused and never used, no quiet restore of a different account, no wallet at all; email wallet restored, Magic login ended, another email signed in). Full suite passes.
- **Not covered:** the naira top-up (GoodCash panel) gets the signed-in address and runs in its own window; if that panel itself asks to connect a wallet, this fix doesn't change it. D122 covers it: the panel now asks our page to sign.
- **UNVERIFIED:** on a real phone with WalletConnect, and how long Magic keeps an email login (set in the Magic dashboard; if it is shorter than our 7-day session, email users see the "sign in again" message after it).
- **Not fixed here: a new address after Disconnect and email sign-in again.** Code can't cause it: Magic gives one fixed wallet per email for one publishable key, and GoodBuild keys accounts by address, so a different address means a different email (a typo, a dot or a `+` in a Gmail address) or a different Magic key on the server. Needs the two addresses and the Magic dashboard to tell which.

### D121. A file attached in the chat goes with the message and shows in its bubble
- **Date:** 2026-11-18. **Decided by:** Claude, from the user's bug report (an attached image stayed as a chip above the composer after sending, so it looked like it hadn't gone through).
- **Cause:** the composer listed every file uploaded to the project, always, and messages didn't record which files went with them.
- **The fix:**
  - The turns API takes `assetIds` (only the project's own files, only on a new message) and saves them on the user message (`meta.assetIds`). The client sends the files waiting in the composer with the next message, whatever sends it (typed, a suggestion chip, resend).
  - The sent bubble shows those files above the text: image thumbnails, other files as a named chip. It shows at once (optimistic), before the server answers.
  - The composer's chips are only files uploaded since the last message (`pending`, worked out in `src/lib/attachments.ts`). Files sent earlier are behind a small "Files N" button next to attach, where they can still be removed or re-classified. A plan answer can't carry files, so a chip waits for the next real message.
  - Older projects: a file uploaded before the newest message counts as sent, so it doesn't reappear above the composer; older bubbles show no files, since that wasn't recorded.
- **Unchanged:** the builder still sees every project file on every turn; this is about what the chat shows.
- **Tests:** `src/lib/attachments.test.ts`, `composer-files.test.tsx`, one in `jobs.test.ts`. Full suite passes.

### D122. GoodCash top-ups: our page signs the permit with the signed-in wallet (18 Nov 2026; decided by the owner with GoodCash's engineer; updates D81)
- **Problem.** On a phone, "Buy G$" opened GoodCash's panel and asked people to connect a wallet again. The panel runs on `pay.goodcash.xyz` and can't see the sign-in our app already did. A phone browser has no wallet extension, so for a Magic email wallet the connect screen had nothing to offer. Signing in to GoodCash by email wouldn't fix it either: Magic would make a different wallet there, and the G$ would land somewhere our app doesn't show.
- **What.** With `GOODCASH_HOST_SIGNING=true` and widget.js 0.4.0 or newer, `BuyDialog` passes `wallet` (the session wallet) and `signTypedData` to `GoodCash.open`. The widget then skips its connect screen. When the order needs approval, it asks our page to sign, and the wallet the person is already using signs (`src/components/buy/host-signing.ts`). We hold no keys (CLAUDE.md rule 8): the person's own wallet signs, exactly as for deploys.
- **What is signed (GoodCash, 18 Nov 2026, and widget.js 0.4.0).** One gasless USDC `ReceiveWithAuthorization` (EIP-3009) on Base. Domain: "USD Coin", version 2, chain 8453, USDC `0x833589fCD6eDb6E08f4c7C32D4f71b54bdA02913`. It moves at most this order's dollars to one of Relay's two approval contracts (`0xccc88a9d1b4ed6b0eaba998850414b24f1c315be`, `0x5567954cd026cebfe486062cb75848e0f1c12e58`). Relay then delivers G$ or CELO to the same wallet on Celo. There is no network fee for the person. GoodCash gives at least 7 days' notice before the Relay list changes.
- **Our check, before the wallet is asked.** Our page refuses unless:
  - the request is exactly that type, with those six fields and nothing extra;
  - `from` and the widget's `wallet` are the session wallet, and the connected wallet is that same address (read when the request arrives, so an account switched in MetaMask is caught);
  - `to` is one of Relay's two contracts;
  - `value` is above 0 and not above the order's `maxUsdc`. A request with no `maxUsdc` or no `orderId` is refused;
  - `validBefore` is in the future and at most 40 minutes away (GoodCash uses 30, plus room for a phone clock), and `validAfter` has already started;
  - the nonce is 32 bytes.

  It then signs a permit rebuilt from the checked fields, never the object it was handed, and only passes on a 65-byte signature. GoodCash's widget and server run their own checks too, including the per-order cap.
- **Base in the wallet.** When `signTypedData` is passed, widget.js does not switch networks. So `useAuth().signTypedDataOn(chain, typedData)` moves the wallet to Base if it can, signs, and always moves it back to the chain it was on, even if the person refuses. Magic's EVM extension now registers Base (8453), and its RPC host `mainnet.base.org` must be allowed in the Magic dashboard. WalletConnect lists Base as an optional chain. A wallet that can't switch signs without switching.
- **If the signature fails after the naira arrived.** The USDC stays in the person's wallet on Base, and nothing is taken. GoodCash's panel offers the last step again for up to 14 days, on the same wallet, with a fresh price each time. The dialog says so once the naira has arrived.
- **The wallet comes back first (D120).** Before opening the panel with host signing, the dialog calls `ensureWallet()`, so a phone that lost its wallet after a reload reconnects the session's own wallet instead of falling back to GoodCash's connect screen. A different account, or a Magic email login that ended, stops with D120's message. `signTypedDataOn` also goes through it, so it never signs with another account.
- **Unchanged.** With the switch off, an older widget, or a connected wallet that isn't the session wallet, the options are exactly as before and the panel shows its own connect screen. Cash out and goodcash.xyz are untouched.
- **Tested.** Unit tests cover the check (each wrong field, type, time, address and amount) and that the rebuilt permit's signature recovers to the wallet. They also cover switching to Base and back, including after a refusal and on a wallet that can't switch. Typecheck, lint and the full suite pass.
- **UNVERIFIED, needs the live test.** Whether Magic's email wallet signs a Base permit on a phone. GoodCash couldn't test this, and test keys skip signing entirely. The only real check is the smallest live top-up (₦1,000) on a phone with a Magic email wallet. Before that, allow `mainnet.base.org` in the Magic dashboard. Report what happens at the signing step to GoodCash.

### D123. Season 5: free credits only for GoodDollar-verified people, plus capped daily refills; unverified people can plan for free (supersedes D28 and D30, amends D29 and D49; numbered D112 at first, renumbered because main took D112 to D122 for games)

- **Decided by:** the owner, 17 Nov 2026, with the weekly limit changed to 5 and the welcome cut to 40 credits on 18 Nov (no free credits without GoodDollar verification, a bigger grant for verified people, daily refills with a weekly reset and a monthly limit, ledger grants not on-chain minting, free planning for the unverified). The amounts and the per-day, per-week and per-month numbers are Claude's proposal. Every amount is a setting. Flow mock: https://claude.ai/artifact/FfoVGAPT5tPFoAYxHa9vPf
- **Planning is free for everyone,** verified or not: up to `FREE_PLAN_TURNS_PER_DAY` (default 5) plan turns a day per account, run as `free` jobs. Unverified accounts get 0 free build credits. Builds, fixes and uploads ask them to verify or buy credits.
- **Welcome grant:** 40 credits ($0.40, `WELCOME_CREDITS`; owner cut it from 100 on 18 Nov: "way less, but enough for a few builds") once per GoodDollar identity, as a ledger `adjust` entry with key `welcome:<root>` (the hackathon pattern, D99). Linked wallets share one root, so a person can only claim it once.
- **Daily refill:** +10 credits (`REFILL_CREDITS`) per claim. The person taps to claim, and nothing is added automatically. Three limits apply together:
  - **Per day:** 1 claim per UTC day. Ledger key `refill:<root>:<YYYY-MM-DD>`.
  - **Per week:** at most 5 claims (`REFILLS_PER_WEEK`, owner, 18 Nov). The count resets on Monday 00:00 UTC, and unused claims don't carry over (owner: "nothing piles up if they don't claim").
  - **Per month:** at most 5 claims (`REFILLS_PER_MONTH`) per calendar month (UTC). When it's reached, no more refills until the 1st.
  - With both caps, the most a person can get in free refills is 50 credits a month. With 5 a week and 5 a month, the monthly limit is the one that bites: someone can claim 5 days in a row, then nothing until the 1st. The weekly limit is kept as a setting so it can be lowered later.
  - Credits already claimed don't expire. They're ordinary balance.
- **Who counts as verified:** only the chain decides, as in D30. The server reads `getWhitelistedRoot(wallet)` on GoodDollar's production Identity contract (`0xC361A6E67822a0EDc17D899227dd9FC50BD62F42`, Celo mainnet). A non-zero result is the person's root, so an already-verified wallet is recognised at sign-in with no face check. GoodDollar's redirect is only a hint. After it, the server re-reads the chain, retrying for about 30 s.
- **Refills need a current verification.** Each claim re-reads the root and checks `lastAuthenticated(root) + authenticationPeriod()` (in days) is still in the future. If it has lapsed, refills pause and the card asks the person to re-verify. The welcome grant has no such check, because it is a one-off.
- **Budget:** D29's all-time total becomes a monthly budget for grants and free planning (`FREE_BUDGET_CREDITS_PER_MONTH`, 5,000 credits = $50 a month, set by the owner on 18 Nov 2026). It counts grants posted this month, free AI charged since the policy started, and 1 credit held for each free plan turn queued or running. When it's spent, claims pause with "This month's free credits are gone" until the 1st. Bought credits are unaffected.
- **Moving to ledger grants (replaces the D28 and D30 counters):** free credit becomes real balance, spent like any other credit, so the free-first logic of D48 and D49 shrinks to "exempt, then balance". Accounts already verified get the welcome grant minus the free usage they've already had (at least 0). Accounts that aren't verified keep any free usage history, but get no new free credits.
- **Why not mint on-chain:** a mint costs gas on every claim and adds a contract call that can fail. A ledger grant shows in the balance the same way.
- **Abuse:** grants and refills run under one advisory lock per root, the same as hackathon claims. A root is read from the chain at claim time, never from the browser. Free planning is limited per account, and new wallets cost nothing to create, so it's the only free AI an unverified wallet gets. It is also counted in the monthly budget.
- **Measured:** `authenticationPeriod()` on the production Identity contract returned **180** (read from forno.celo.org, 18 Nov 2026). GoodDollar's docs say it is in days, so a verification lasts about six months.
- **As built (18 Nov 2026, Claude):**
  - `src/server/credits/free-grants.ts`: settings, the pure `refillStatus` (day, week and month windows in UTC), `claimWelcome`, `claimRefill`, `freeStatus` and the monthly budget. No migration: grants are ledger `adjust` entries with `ref_type` `welcome` or `refill` and `ref_id` the root. Refills are counted from their keys (`refill:<root>:<day>`), so the day a claim counts for is fixed when it's made.
  - `src/server/goodid.ts`: `readGoodIdentity` adds `lastAuthenticated` and `authenticationPeriod`. If either read fails, the expiry is unknown and nothing is refused for it.
  - `src/server/credits/cap.ts`: under `FREE_POLICY=verified` (the default) the only `free` AI is plan turns. Builds, fixes, uploads and visual checks are paid from the balance, with the D49 holds. `freeRemainingMicro` is 0, and every unverified account is offered verification.
  - `POST /api/account/goodid` posts the welcome as soon as the chain shows a root, so verifying is the whole step. `GET /api/credits/free` returns the card's state with no chain read. `POST /api/credits/free/refill` re-reads the chain, then claims.
  - `FreeCreditsCard` (Credits page, and the workspace in compact form) is the verify card for unverified people and the refill card with a 5-slot month meter for verified ones. When it loads it checks the chain once, quietly, so someone already verified on GoodDollar gets the welcome without pressing anything.
  - Landing, pricing, docs, the credits pill and the blog say "planning is free, 40 credits when verified" instead of "20 credits free".
  - **`FREE_POLICY=legacy`** brings back D28 and D30 unchanged. The tests that pin those rules now run under it.
- **Why 40 and not lower:** live builds cost about 2 to 35 credits (D28), so 40 covers a few small ones. It must stay at least `PAID_MIN_START_MICRO` (now 10 credits), or the welcome couldn't start a build.
- **The minimum to start a build is now 10 credits (owner, 18 Nov 2026: "lower the minimum so refills work").** `PAID_MIN_START_MICRO` went from 350,000 to 100,000, one refill, so a person with only a refill can build. This amends D49. The checks before each model call still keep spending safe. The trade-off the owner accepted: a fast-model call is at most about 5 credits, but a strong-model fix on a large project can need up to about 35. On a small balance that check fails, and the build stops with "your credits ran out" after spending what it had. The smallest purchase stays at 35 credits (`MIN_PURCHASE_MICRO`), which no longer follows the build minimum.
- **Pre-D112 free use and the welcome:** free AI used before `FREE_POLICY_START` (default 18 Nov 2026 00:00 UTC) by any account on the same root comes off the welcome. Set it to the real deploy time, or free use between this date and the deploy isn't counted.
- **Checked:** `free-grants.test.ts` (19 tests: the week, month and month-crossing windows, unverified refused, welcome once and once per root, earlier free use deducted, daily, weekly and monthly limits shared across linked accounts, expiry, budget, the legacy switch, and the funding: builds refused or paid from grants with a hold, plans free up to the daily limit and inside the budget), on PGlite and real Postgres 16. Also `goodid.test.ts` (expiry maths and unknown expiry), `free-credits-card.test.tsx` (verify, quiet detection, claim, refusal, compact and legacy hiding), every existing credits test under the legacy switch, and the full suite. The Credits page was opened signed in on a local server at 1280 px and 390 px, with no sideways scroll.
- **Refills need today's G$ UBI claimed (owner, 18 Nov 2026, Claude's option A; the stricter "required" form, to spend less).** GoodDollar's UBI contract on Celo is `0x43d72Ff17701B2DA814620735C39C620Ce0ea4A1` (UBIScheme, "production-celo" in GoodProtocol's `releases/deployment.json`). Read live on 18 Nov 2026: `periodStart` 2023-03-01T12:00:00Z, about 122.5 G$ per claim, about 9,200 claimers that day. A verified person can claim once per GoodDollar day, and linked wallets share the claim.
  - **The rule:** a refill is paid only if `lastClaimed(root)` on that contract is at or after the start of GoodDollar's current day. The server reads it itself, and the browser's word is never trusted. `REFILL_REQUIRES_UBI=off` drops the rule.
  - **Refill days follow GoodDollar's day,** noon to noon UTC, so "claim G$, then claim your refill" is one day in both places. Ledger keys use the date the GoodDollar day began on. Refill weeks start Monday at noon UTC, and refill months start at noon UTC on the 1st. The monthly budget stays on calendar months.
  - **One tap:** the card's "Claim G$ + 10 credits" asks the wallet to send `claim()` to the UBI contract on Celo mainnet (switching network if needed), waits for it to be mined, then claims the refill, retrying for up to about 10 s while the chain catches up. If GoodDollar's `checkEntitlement` says nothing is due (already claimed today from any linked wallet), it sends nothing. `GET /api/credits/free` reads the claim once per load for a verified person with a refill available, so the button can say "Claim 10 credits" when the G$ is already claimed.
  - **The cost:** the person pays a small CELO network fee. Someone with no CELO on Celo, such as a new email-login wallet, can't claim and is told so. There's no fallback, which is the conservative choice.
  - Checked against the live contract (read-only): `claim()` from an unverified address reverts with "UBIScheme: not whitelisted", and `checkEntitlement(address)` returns today's amount even for an unverified address, so it isn't used as proof of anything.
- **Five more limits to spend less (owner, 18 Nov 2026, from Claude's list):** every one is a setting, and 0 turns off the ones that say so.
  - **Refill only when low:** the claiming account must have under `REFILL_BELOW_CREDITS` (10) credits. Free credit can't pile up, and people with credits get no more.
  - **Weekly budget:** `FREE_BUDGET_CREDITS_PER_WEEK` (1,250, a quarter of the month's 5,000) on calendar weeks from Monday 00:00 UTC. Whichever of week and month is tighter decides, and the refusal says when it comes back.
  - **Active builders only:** a refill needs a finished build or a deploy within `REFILL_ACTIVE_DAYS` (30) on any account with the same GoodDollar root. Plans don't count.
  - **Fewer free plans before verifying:** `FREE_PLAN_TURNS_PER_DAY_UNVERIFIED` (2) for unverified accounts, which cost nothing to make, and 5 for verified ones.
  - **Free credits expire:** `FREE_CREDITS_EXPIRE_DAYS` (30). `src/server/credits/free-expiry.ts` runs hourly in `pnpm worker`, as the hackathon expiry does (D100). Free credit counts as spent first, so what's left of it came from the newest grants. Per account, it removes what's left beyond the grants inside the window. "Left" counts every spend since the first grant, paid ones too, so an error keeps credit with the person. It never touches more than the balance not set aside for builds, never takes bought credit, and recomputes from the ledger under the account's balance lock each run. Entries are `adjust` with `ref_type` `free_expiry`, and the budget doesn't get them back.
  - The low-balance and activity checks run before the wallet is asked to claim G$, on the server and in the card, so nobody pays a network fee for a refill they can't have. The low-balance check runs again under the lock.
- **Fixed while testing (not a free-credit change):** `ensureHold` didn't check whether a build had already ended. If a build was stopped while one of its model calls waited for the lock, the hold extension hit the database trigger ("hold of finished turn job can't change") and threw, instead of stopping cleanly. It now returns false for a job that isn't queued or running, so no more spending happens on it. This was the cause of the occasional failure in the holds race test (about 1 run in 6 on Postgres). After the fix it passed 15 runs in a row, and a new unit test covers it.
- **UNVERIFIED:** a real UBI claim followed by a refill from a verified wallet. Also that `lastAuthenticated` is in seconds (no verified wallet was read), a real face check end to end with a Magic email wallet (carried over from D30), and a refill claim against a real verified wallet.

### D124. Gas grant goes live for the hackathon vote: 0.3 CELO, once per person, 50 a day (18 Nov 2026; amends D41)
- Decided by: the owner (amount and daily limit), built by Claude with the advisor's review.
- **What:** when a GoodDollar-verified person who has FlowState voting power reviews their vote and their wallet can't pay the fee cap, the server tops the wallet up to **0.3 CELO** from the gas wallet (`0x26b0…24cb`), waits for it to land, then shows the review with "We added X CELO". One grant per GoodID root, at most **50 a day**. The owner meant 0.3 to cover both the vote and their first deploy, so it replaces D41's 1 CELO; the `reason` column is ready for the deploy path (G1), which is not wired yet.
- **What 0.3 buys (18 Nov, read from chain):** the vote costs about 0.064 CELO (about 313k gas at the 200 gwei floor). That leaves about 0.24 CELO: two or three small contracts, **not** one large 24 kB contract (about 1.3 CELO, MAINNET_PLAN section 7).
- **Fund:** 18.97 CELO on 18 Nov, so about 63 people before a refill. The admin page shows the balance; the owner's support Telegram chat is told when fewer than 5 grants are left, when it is empty, and when a top-up needs review.
- **Why a top-up, not sponsorship:** FlowCouncil's `vote` uses `msg.sender` with no forwarder or signed-vote path (verified source on Blockscout), so nobody else can pay for or send the vote. Fee abstraction needs a fee token the person doesn't hold.
- **Safety, from MAINNET_PLAN section 7:** no address in the request, two fresh GoodDollar reads, unique root, daily count under a lock, signing under the same lock with nonces never reused, signed transaction saved before broadcast and only ever resent, stuck after 10 minutes goes to `needs_review` and is never retried, kill switch `app_settings.gas_grants = 'off'`, no imports from credits, Paystack or GoodCash (tested).
- **The key:** read from `/run/secrets/gas_wallet_key`, mounted read-only into web only (`deploy/install-gas-key.sh`). **Still to do by the owner:** remove `GAS_WALLET_PRIVATE_KEY` from `.env.production`, because today web and worker receive it as an environment variable, which section 7 point 8 forbids. Web holding the key is the owner's accepted interim instead of the separate sender container section 7 recommends.
- **Also fixed:** the vote's tip was set equal to the whole gas price, doubling what people paid (about 0.13 instead of 0.064 CELO). It is now Celo's suggested tip over a doubled base fee.

### D125. The welcome credits also need a FlowState vote for GoodBuildIt (18 Nov 2026; amends D123)
- Decided by: the owner ("add voters", then: the welcome waits for the vote, and the vote happens on the platform). Built by Claude.
- **What:** D123's 40-credit welcome is posted only once the person's wallet has settled support for GoodBuildIt on the FlowState GoodBuilders council, read from the chain (`hasVotedForGoodBuild`: support above 0, unchanged for 2 blocks). Daily refills are unchanged: they need verification only.
- **Where the vote happens:** in GoodBuild's own vote dialog (`FlowstateVote`, the hackathon one), shown by the free-credits card as "Unlock 40 free credits" once someone is verified but not yet welcomed. The network fee is covered by the gas grant (D124), so a new email wallet with 0 CELO can still do it.
- **Who it doesn't touch:** anyone already welcomed is never asked again. `WELCOME_REQUIRES_VOTE=off` brings back verify-only welcomes, for example if FlowState's contract changes (the vote check fails closed, so no welcome is posted while it can't be read).
- **Side effect:** none since D126: voting no longer enrols anyone in the hackathon.
- **Not changed:** landing, pricing and docs still say "40 credits when verified". The card itself says the vote is part of it.

### D126. Any signed-in wallet can vote; voting doesn't enrol anyone in the hackathon (18 Nov 2026; amends D125)
- Decided by: the owner (email sign-in and imported wallets follow the same rules: verify and vote for free credits). Built by Claude.
- **What:** the vote dialog's API keeps its own per-account record (`flowstate_voters`: the 90-second eligibility rate limit, the vote's tx hash and time) instead of creating a hackathon participant row, which needed an email. A wallet with no email on its account can now vote and unlock the welcome credits.
- **Hackathon signups:** if the person has already registered for the hackathon, the vote is copied onto their participant row too, so the CSV export and the hackathon credit claim keep working. Voters who never registered aren't in the CSV.
- **Agreed next (not built yet):** a sign-in pop-up for people with 0 credits and no purchase (Add credits / Get free credits: verify, then "support our project by voting"); hackathon registration requiring verify and vote; and in-app voting on other people's hackathon projects, open to verified voters.

### D127. Celo mainnet is the wallet default; Celo Sepolia only when someone picks it (18 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner, after a person pressing "Verify with GoodDollar" on /hackathon saw MetaMask ask to add Celo Sepolia. Built by Claude.
- **Why:** Verify only asks for a signature, but older wallet code treated the testnet as the default, so MetaMask had a leftover "Add Celo Sepolia" request queued in front of it.
- **What:** MetaMask sign-in asks permission for Celo mainnet only (Ethereum as the fallback when the wallet has no Celo); WalletConnect requires Celo mainnet, with Celo Sepolia optional; `celoChainFor` returns Celo mainnet for any chain other than Celo Sepolia. Picking the testnet in the wallet menu, or deploying a testnet project, still adds and switches to Celo Sepolia.

### D128. One-button vote, locked signup email, and a Hackathon page in the app (18 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner. Built by Claude.
- **Vote pop-up:** a big points number, a thick slider, and one "Vote N points" button. The button switches the wallet to Celo, asks the server to price the vote (covering the network fee if needed, D124), then opens the wallet's own confirmation. The review screen, fee table, explanations and contract details are gone. The labelled button plus the wallet's prompt are the consent. Kept: enable voting, "I already voted", not-verified, the pending-vote check and recovery, and the Telegram/CELO help.
- **Signup email:** once known (the account's email or the first one saved), it's shown masked and blurred and can't be edited. The server keeps the first email saved, so a later request can't change it either. A wallet-only account types it once.
- **Hackathon in the app:** `/home/hackathon` (sidebar item, plus a wallet-menu link for phones) has progress (verify, vote, credits), submit or update your project, and approve other projects. The public `/hackathon` page's "Submit or update your project" now opens it.

### D129. Voters get their 40 without asking; the vote button always shows; an out-of-credits pop-up (18 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner, after hackathon people who had verified and voted still saw "vote first", never got the 40, and a G$ top-up looked stuck. Built by Claude.
- **Why:** nobody had received the welcome credits (0 `welcome` rows in production). The free-credits card never re-checked a verified person's vote, and voting on /hackathon never claimed the welcome. The vote button used a colour defined only on the /hackathon page, so it was invisible elsewhere. The G$ payment was credited; the screen just waited for the 30-block confirmation (GB-13) with no explanation.
- **What:**
  - A confirmed vote (`/api/hackathon/flowstate` confirm) claims the welcome straight away, once per GoodID, and returns the amount. Opening the free-credits card also checks the vote quietly for a verified person and adds the 40 if they've voted.
  - Hackathon people get the 40 on top of their 20 hackathon credits (60 total).
  - The vote pop-up checks for an existing vote on opening and never asks someone who voted to vote again. The vote button has a fallback colour.
  - While a G$ payment confirms, the button reads "Confirming payment…" with "usually about 30 seconds". The 30-block wait stays.
  - When a build stops for lack of credits, a pop-up opens: pay in G$ (exact amount, one click) or get free credits (only the step that person still needs), then "Continue building" retries. Closing it leaves the inline pill.

### D130. Hackathon runs 18 Nov to 10 Dec 2026, one capped 1,000,000 G$ pool, top 20 paid, credits stay usable (18 Nov 2026; replaces the Jan to Feb draft and the D100 expiry)
- Decided by: the owner. Built by Claude.
- **Dates:** registration, submissions and voting all open 18 Nov and close together at the end of 10 Dec (`CLAIMS_CLOSE` = `2026-12-10T23:00:00Z`). Builders are told to submit by 5 Dec for a fair vote window. Winners announced 12 Dec (proposed). The old build-start, mid-point and demo-day stages are gone.
- **Rewards (`src/lib/hackathon-rewards.ts`):** a fixed 1,000,000 G$ pool that is never exceeded. 700,000 G$ in rank prizes for the top 20 published projects (175k, 105k, 70k, 42k x2, 21k x5, 16.1k x10). The other 300,000 G$ is shared by approvals at `min(5,000, 300,000 / total approvals)` G$ each, rounded down, on every published project. Fewer than 20 eligible entries leave unused rank prizes unpaid. Earnings are estimates until the close.
- **Replaces:** ten equal 100,000 G$ slots and an uncapped 50,000 G$ per approval funded outside the pool.
- **Credits:** unspent hackathon credits are no longer expired. The worker's hourly sweep is removed. The manual admin button and `/api/hackathon/expire` remain but nothing runs them.
- **Built:** the live ladder (`ladder.tsx`, shared by `/hackathon` and the app). **Open:** the winners and payout dates; quests, voter badges, weekly Rising Star and referral are planned, not built.

### D131. Get-credits pop-up on home; verify and vote before submitting a hackathon project (18 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner (agreed in D126, built now). Built by Claude.
- **Pop-up:** someone who lands on home with no credits and has never bought any (no deposit in the ledger) sees "Get credits to start building": Add credits, or the free credits card, which shows only the step they still need (verify, then "support our project by voting"; the 40 lands when the vote confirms). It shows once per browser session. A failed check never shows it.
- **Hackathon:** claiming the hackathon credits already needed verify and a confirmed vote. Submitting or updating a project now needs them too: the server refuses until the signup's vote is confirmed on-chain (only that check sets "voted", and it requires a verified wallet). The form shows "Verify and vote first" with a link to the steps. No project in production was affected (none submitted yet).

### D132. Hackathon in the app becomes four pages; three approvals per verified person; public vote section (19 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner. Built by Claude.
- **App (`/home/hackathon`):** Get started, My project, Leaderboard, Vote, each its own page under a shared layout with tabs. People who have registered see a "you're in" summary on Get started (credits, claim the 30 after a deploy, links to the other tabs) and never see the four steps again. My project holds the submit form. Leaderboard is the shared ladder.
- **Voting rule:** each verified person (one GoodDollar identity, so linked wallets share) has **3 approvals in total** in this hackathon, replacing "one per project, no limit". Approving the same project again is a no-op. The count and insert run under a per-identity advisory lock, so parallel requests can't pass three. Existing approvals count toward the three; nobody loses any, but anyone already over three can't add more.
- **Eligibility, enforced on the server:** a verified GoodDollar identity **and** a confirmed on-chain FlowState vote (`flowstate_voters.voted_at`). A hackathon signup is not needed, so people who only vote can still approve (D126).
- **Public `/hackathon`:** a Vote section, and `/hackathon/projects` uses the same list. Picking a project when the steps aren't done opens a small pop-up (connect email, verify with GoodDollar, support on FlowState), then one click to approve. The picked project survives the GoodDollar trip (session storage, return to `#vote`).
- **New:** `GET /api/hackathon/votes` (steps done, approvals used and which), `src/lib/hackathon-vote.ts`, `vote-list.tsx`, `get-started.tsx`, `home-tabs.tsx`. The old gallery in `community.tsx` is removed.
- **Not verified in a browser:** the dev server wouldn't run in this environment (Turbopack file watching is blocked by the sandbox, and the webpack fallback chokes on Tailwind's native module). Server rules are covered by tests; the pages are typechecked and linted only.

### D133. Quests and badges on Get started (19 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner (asked for quests next). Built by Claude.
- **What:** a quest list and badge row for registered builders on `/home/hackathon` (`quests.tsx`, rules in `src/lib/hackathon-quests.ts`). Six quests: register (+20 credits), deploy (+30 credits), publish, use your 3 approvals, get 5 approvals (Bronze), reach the top 20. Badges: First approval, Full ballot, Published, Bronze, Silver, Gold, Prize zone.
- **No new tables, no new money.** Everything is read from what exists: the registration claim, `GET /api/hackathon/votes`, and the public entry list. The only credits are the 20 + 30 the existing claims already pay. Badges never pay G$ (no incentive to farm votes).
- **Changed from the preview:** the "Talent spotter (5 approvals)" badge is gone, because D132 caps approvals at 3. "Scout" (approved a project that finished top 20) needs final rankings, so it is not built.
- **League levels (owner, 19 Nov):** Bronze 5, Silver 15, Gold 30 approvals received, lowered from 10/40/100 because each person has only 3 approvals (about 300 in total at 100 builders). One constant, `LEAGUE_AT` in `src/lib/hackathon-ladder.ts`, also drives the leaderboard chips.
- **Not built:** weekly Rising Star (needs a snapshot job), referral credits (new rules and tables), rank-movement arrows (need a daily rank snapshot).

### D134. Leaderboard rank arrows from a daily snapshot (19 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner (picked rank arrows next). Built by Claude.
- **What:** each published project shows "▲ 3" or "▼ 2" next to its name on the leaderboard: places gained or lost since the last day's snapshot. No arrow for a project with no earlier snapshot, or no change.
- **How:** new table `hackathon_rank_snapshots` (migration `0026`). The worker (`scripts/worker.ts`, the separate container in production) calls `sweepRankSnapshot` hourly; the first run of each West Africa day writes that day's ranks and later runs change nothing. `GET /api/hackathon/projects` adds `prevRank` from the latest snapshot dated before today. Snapshots run through the day after the close (the final ranking) and then stop.
- **Limits:** the snapshot is taken at the worker's first run each WAT day, normally within an hour of midnight, so the arrow means "since about midnight". With no worker running (local PGlite) there are no arrows. The first arrows appear the day after the first snapshot.
- **Migration number:** `0026` was the next free number on main on 19 Nov. If another branch adds `0026` first, renumber one of them.
- **Not built:** weekly Rising Star (can reuse these snapshots), referral credits.

### D135. Weekly Rising Star badge (19 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner (picked Rising Star next). Built by Claude. The three weeks are the ones from the preview and are Claude's proposal until confirmed.
- **What:** the published project with the most new approvals in each week gets a "★ Rising Star" badge on the leaderboard and on its owner's badge row. Weeks: 18 to 25 Nov, 26 Nov to 2 Dec, 3 to 10 Dec. No money, so a project far down the ladder can still win. Ties go to the earlier submission; a week with no new approvals has no winner.
- **How:** derived from the daily rank snapshots (D134), no new tables. A week ends at the snapshot dated the day after it (26 Nov, 3 Dec, 11 Dec), so the first star appears on 26 Nov. `src/lib/hackathon-weeks.ts` holds the rules; `GET /api/hackathon/projects` adds `stars` (week numbers) per project.
- **Limits:** needs the worker to have written the boundary-day snapshot. If it missed a day, the next snapshot is used; if a week can't be measured it is skipped, not guessed.
- **Not built:** referral credits.


### D136. Referral credits (19 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: the owner (picked referrals next). Built by Claude. The 1,000-credit referral pot and the 3-friend limit were confirmed by the owner (19 Nov).
- **What:** every registered builder has an invite link (`/hackathon?ref=CODE`) on Get started. When someone registers through it and later makes their first Celo deploy, the invited builder and the inviter each get 10 build credits.
- **Rules:** one bonus per GoodDollar identity (keyed on the invited person's root, so a second account can't claim it again). The invited builder is always paid; an inviter is paid for up to 3 friends. Codes are random, never derived from a wallet or user id. A code only counts in the call that creates the registration: someone already registered who opens a link gets nothing. A bad, unknown, self or same-identity code is ignored and registration goes ahead as normal. Nothing is paid after the close (10 Dec).
- **Money:** a separate pot (`HACKATHON_REFERRAL_BUDGET_CREDITS`, default 1,000 credits) checked in the same hackathon lock, so referrals can't use up the 20 start credits promised to new builders. Both bonuses are paid together or not at all. If the pot is short the referral is held, the invited builder sees "Claim your invite bonus" on Get started, and a later claim pays it once the pot is topped up. Ledger entries are `adjust` with per-identity idempotency keys. The admin report shows referral credits paid against that pot.
- **How:** new table `hackathon_referrals` and `referral_code` on registrations (migration `0027`; renumber if another branch takes it). `POST /api/hackathon` takes an optional `{ ref }`; `GET /api/hackathon/referral` returns the code and counts. The link is kept in localStorage from `?ref=` until the person registers.
- **Not verified in a browser** (see D132).

### D137. Record GoodDollar verification wherever the chain confirms it (19 Nov 2026)
- Decided by: Claude, fixing a bug the owner reported (the "Verified with GoodDollar" count on /stats stuck at 5).
- **Cause:** /stats counts `users.verified_at`, which was only written by the Verify button (`POST /api/account/goodid`), the credits quote, welcome/refill grants and the hackathon claim. The hackathon vote dialog, approvals and FlowState vote read verification straight from the chain and never saved it. So anyone already verified with GoodDollar (e.g. in GoodWallet) saw step 2 ticked, never pressed Verify, and was never counted. The welcome grant would have recorded them, but it rolls back if the free-credit budget is full.
- **Fix:** `recordingRootReader` / `recordGoodIdRoot` in `src/server/goodid.ts`. `GET /api/hackathon/votes`, approvals (`POST /api/hackathon/projects`) and every FlowState status check now record the signed-in wallet's own root the first time the chain says it's verified. Only fills in an account not yet verified; a project owner's wallet read during an approval is never recorded against the voter.
- **Past accounts:** `scripts/goodid-backfill.ts` re-reads the chain for every account with no `verified_at` (dry run by default, `--write` to save). Run once on production.
- /stats is also cached for up to 10 minutes, so a new verification can take that long to show.

### D138. A build that runs low on credits fits its calls to what's left, and keeps what it made (19 Nov 2026; amends D19, D49 and D123)
- Decided by: the owner (approved PR 1 of the plan, 19 Nov: "yes, go ahead with PR 1"). Built by Claude.
- **Why:** a real build ("Shadow Crew", a 3D crew-and-saboteur game) stopped with "your credits ran out" while the person still had 22 credits. Its only failing check was the MiniPay kit's MINI_APP placeholder. That fix went to the strong model, whose worst case on a project that size is about 35 credits, so the D49 check refused the call. The build then ended as an error, which saves nothing, so "Try again" re-sent "Start building" and ran the whole plan again, stopping at the same step.
- **What changed:**
  - **Calls are fitted, not refused** (`fitCall`, `headroomMicro` in `src/server/credits/holds.ts`; used by `fitToCredit` in the runner). Before each paid build, fix or wrap-up call: as asked if its worst case fits the hold plus the free balance; else on the fast model; else with a lower output limit, down to 4,000 tokens (`MIN_FITTED_OUTPUT_TOKENS`). Only when not even that fits does the build pause. The worst case is still D49's estimate and the hold check still runs on the fitted call, so the balance still can't go below zero. Side calls (the review's screenshots) are not fitted. The saved call keeps the tier it asked for, so a resumed build replays it as before. The build log says when a step was moved to the fast model or limited.
  - **A build that pauses for credits keeps its files** (`saveDraft` in the runner). It is saved as a version like a build that ends with errors (`needs_fix`), with `paused: "credits"` and `CREDITS_PAUSED_MESSAGE` on the record. The preview and code show the draft; the card reads "Paused: your credits ran low" with **Continue**, which sends `CONTINUE_PROMPT` (pick up from the saved files) instead of resending the first prompt. A build that wrote nothing still ends with the old message. Stop pressed by the person still discards the turn's edits (unchanged, the person's choice).
  - **GoodBuild fills MINI_APP from the plan** (`fillMiniApp` in the MiniPay kit, `miniAppInfo` in the runner): a game's name, the first sentence of its pitch, and three plain steps; otherwise the project's name and the plan's summary. Only the kit's own placeholder text is replaced, never something the builder or person wrote. Saved in the turn setup as `appInfo` (older saves don't have it and skip this).
  - **Missing wiring and placeholders get the fast model first** (`fixTier`): a first fix attempt whose errors are all `paths` or `types` issues goes to the cheap tier, like test-only failures (D19). Later attempts still go to the strong one.
  - The scripted test model now caps output at the call's limit, as a real provider does.
- **Trade-offs:** a fix moved to the fast model is weaker than the strong model's; the person sees it in the log and can Continue after a top-up. A shrunk output limit can cut a big file short; the existing "write it in smaller pieces" retry handles that, and each retry is fitted again.
- **PR 2 (same day):** the paused card says what Continue costs (the build estimate) against what the person has, in the warning colour when it's short, and the button reads "Continue · ≈ N". The composer's estimate turns warning-coloured with "you have N, it may pause" when a build's estimate is above what the person can spend; the build still starts, since it now pauses and saves instead of failing. Paying from the out-of-credits dialog after a paused build sends Continue, not the first prompt again.
- **PR 3 (same day):**
  - **More build passes when the plan isn't finished** (`soundsUnfinished`, `MORE_BUILD_PASSES` in the runner). When a build pass ends with a reply that says work is left ("it isn't finished yet", "still to come are...", "the next pass will add..."), the runner asks the same model to keep building, up to 2 more passes, before the checks. The person reads the last summary, not the "not finished" one. A pass that adds no new reply stops it. Each pass is fitted to the credit left; free builds still stop at their ceiling. **Changed from the plan:** I said it would sit behind a setting until real builds confirmed it. It ships on, because off it fixes nothing for the person who asked; `MORE_BUILD_PASSES=0` turns it off and the owner can do that from the environment. A reply that only mentions the person's own next steps ("deploy the contract to post scores") doesn't trigger it.
  - **A quiet model stream is retried, not waited on** (`MODEL_IDLE_MS`, default 2 minutes, in `aiSdkClient`). A stream that sends nothing at all (no text, thinking or tool input) for that long ends as ModelUnavailableError, which the runner already retries once. Before this a stalled provider socket left the build on "Thinking" with no limit. The cause of the person's own "Thinking for minutes" isn't known (no worker log was available); this bounds it either way.
  - **The chat says how long it's been quiet.** "Thinking" now has its clock whenever no reply has shown yet, restarting at each new step or log line (it used to stop once the first step appeared), and after 90 seconds of quiet a calm note says it is still working and that a long quiet step is retried.
  - **UNVERIFIED** with a real model; whether real builders' "not finished" replies match the phrases is the thing to watch in the worker log (`the builder said the plan isn't finished`).
- **UNVERIFIED** with a real model and on the live site. Tested with the scripted model on PGlite (`holds.test.ts`, `runner.test.ts`, `mini-app.test.ts`); the Postgres race tests skip without a database.

### D139. Templates only when the gameplay matches; a multiplayer room, a dealer and more genre templates (19 Nov 2026; amends D114)
- Decided by: the owner (approved the plan below, 19 Nov, including adding an app-data dealer). Built by Claude, one PR at a time.
- **Why:** someone asked for a 3D multiplayer "crew vs saboteur" game (rooms with a join code, wallet sign-in, voting, roles). The planner started it from the one-player 3D maze template (`maze-escape`) because both are 3D with corridors, but the two share almost nothing, so the build used up its steps rebuilding the template and stopped half-done.
- **The plan, in order (one PR each, merged when CI is green):**
  1. **PR 4 (this one): a template only when a round plays the same way.** The brief's `template` field now says to pick one only with the same controls, goal and way to win or lose, never for a shared camera, setting or way of moving, and "none" when in doubt. The catalogue line says whether each template is one player or a room. A code guard in `parseGameBrief` (`isMultiplayerGame` in `plan-intake.ts`) drops any one-player template for a multiplayer or social game: online or multiplayer play, real people, rooms, lobbies and join codes, voting someone out, an imposter, saboteur or traitor, hidden roles, social deduction, or two or more players. It reads the request and the planner's own name, pitch, loop and lose lines. Such a game starts from scratch until a multiplayer template fits it. `GameTemplate.multiplayer?: boolean` marks the templates that may start one (none yet).
  2. **PR 5: a multiplayer room template and two games on it.** A shared lobby: wallet sign-in, create a room with a short code, others join by code, ready up, the host starts, rematch. Turns are worked out from records as the multiplayer guide says (polling, no live connection). Games: Four-in-a-row (2 players) and Trivia battle (2 to 8 players, the same questions for everyone, live scores). Original names and art.
  3. **PR 6: an app-data dealer.** A platform change: a room's host asks GoodBuild's server to shuffle and deal, and each player's role or hand is written so only that player can read it, not even the host. Designed in APP_DATA.md and its own decision first, with security tests that no other player, the host, or any filter or sort query can read another player's deal. The D34 access model stays as it is. Then the multiplayer guide learns the dealer.
  4. **PR 7: a saboteur party game** on the room and the dealer: an original crew vs hidden saboteur game in phases (tasks, sabotage, a meeting when someone is knocked out, a vote), 4 to 10 players. Never Among Us's name, characters or art.
  5. **PR 8: card games.** One player: solitaire (Klondike rules, original look), higher-or-lower, memory pairs. Multiplayer: an original shedding game in the crazy-eights style, on the dealer. No gambling for money (D4, D72).
  6. **PR 9 and later:** platformer, tower defense, rhythm tap, block stacker, word grid, idle clicker, one or two per PR.
- **Trade-offs:** the words guard is a list, so it can miss a social game described in other words (the field's wording is the first line of defence) or call a game social that isn't (for example "a two-player board game" on one phone). A false call only loses the template; the build starts from scratch, which is what every game did before D114. "Top 10 players" and an escape room are not counted.
- **UNVERIFIED** with a real model: whether the planner now picks "none" for such requests by itself. Tested with parsed tool calls only (`plan-first.test.ts`).

### D140. A multiplayer room template, with Four in a row and Trivia battle on it (19 Nov 2026; PR 5 of D139)
- Decided by: the owner (D139's plan). Built by Claude.
- **The room** (`game-templates/room.ts`, written into a project as `src/room/`): wallet sign-in through app data, Make a room (a 5-letter code from letters that can't be misread, unique per app), Join by code, I'm ready, Start the game (host only, once enough are seated and everyone is ready), Rematch (the host takes everyone back to the lobby for the next round), Leave or Close room. Three collections, everyone reads, each person writes only their own records: `rooms` (the host's, key = code: game, status, round, players in turn order, a shared random seed), `seats` (each player's, key = code: name and the round they're ready for) and `moves` (one record per move: round, number `n`, the move as JSON, never edited). Every screen polls every 3 seconds (`useRoom`, `LIVE_MS`) and works out the same state from the records, as the multiplayer guide says: the first record for each turn wins, later ones are shown as too late. People past the room's size watch. `GameTemplate.rules` adds the three rules to `dataRules.json`, leaving a collection the person already has alone.
- **Four in a row** (`four-in-a-row`, shown as "Line Up Four"): 2 players, 7 x 6, turns from the moves (`replay`). **Trivia battle** (`trivia-battle`, shown as "Answer Rush"): 2 to 8 players, the same 10 questions in the same order (the room's seed), each player at their own pace against a 15-second clock, everyone's score live, the round ends when all have answered or at a deadline (so someone who leaves can't stall it). Both are marked `multiplayer: true`, so D139's guard lets the planner pick them for social games.
- **Play the computer** is the first button on both start screens: one player against a computer that wins, blocks and avoids giving a win, or against three computer quiz players. It's for people with nobody online, and it's what GoodBuild's run check plays, since the check has one browser.
- **MiniPay:** rooms aren't offered there (app-data sign-in needs a signature MiniPay refuses); Play the computer works. The MiniPay guardrail skips `src/room/Lobby.tsx` for that reason, as it skips the leaderboard.
- **Tests** (`templates.test.ts`): both pass the app check, the type check and the run check (also with the MiniPay kit); two players (two pages with their own wallets on the preview's shared store) make a room, join by code, ready, start, play to a result shown on both screens and rematch; both play cleanly on a slow touch phone (CPU 4x slower); a copy whose columns do nothing is caught; the room's rules pass `parseDataRules`.
- **Limits:** moves, answers and their timings come from players' own devices, so a result is between friends, never proof for money (D4, D72). A turn takes up to 3 seconds to reach the other screen. A room is found by a query, which reads the newest 500 records of a collection (D79), so in a very busy app an old room code can stop being found. While a round is on, each player makes about 60 queries a minute, half the per-person limit.
- **UNVERIFIED:** on a real phone, and on a published app with real wallet signatures (tested on the preview's in-browser store, which applies the same rules but no signatures). No real model has built from these templates yet.

### D141. An app-data dealer: the server shuffles and deals, and each player's share is theirs alone (19 Nov 2026; PR 6 of D139; extends D31 and D34)
- Decided by: the owner (D139's plan: "a room's host can ask GoodBuild's server to shuffle and deal ... not even the host"). Designed and built by Claude. Design in APP_DATA.md, "The dealer".
- **Why:** hidden roles and card hands can't be fair if a player's device shuffles, and the room's host can't do it either, because whoever shuffles knows the deal. App data could only make a record readable by anyone, its writer or the app's owner, never by one chosen other player.
- **What:**
  - A new rule, `"dealt"`. Its records are written only by the server. Each one is stored under the player it belongs to and saved with the grant `{read: ["writer"], public: []}`. Normal adds, replacements and deletes there are refused (403), for the owner too.
  - `POST deal/<collection>` (signed in with a wallet) takes the players, a deck of short texts and a hand size. The server shuffles with `crypto.randomInt` and writes each player's share. It keeps the rest as a stock nobody can read (`app_data_deals`, migration 0028), and returns only the deal id and counts.
  - `POST deal/<collection>/<id>/draw` lets a player in the deal take from the stock, for them only. It runs under a row lock, so two draws can't take the same item.
  - `GET deal/<collection>/<id>` gives the deal's host and players the counts.
  - The app's helper has `deal()`, `draw()` and `dealInfo()`, with a stand-in in the preview, where nothing is secret and the note says so. The data-rules and multiplayer guides say how to use them.
- **The D34 model is unchanged.** A hand is an ordinary record whose writer is the player, so every existing read path applies as it was. The new checks are only the refusal of writes to dealt collections and the dealer's own routes. An extra flag on `putRecord` lets only the dealer write there.
- **Tests:** `deal.attack.test.ts` (12) and `deal-helper.test.ts` (3); see APP_DATA.md "How it's tested".
- **Limits and trade-offs:**
  - The server doesn't enforce turns. Draw counts are visible to everyone, so a game can show an out-of-turn draw.
  - Players trust GoodBuild's server for a fair shuffle, as for every record. Operators running the database can read hands, as with private records (APP_DATA.md).
  - A stranger could deal junk hands to a player. The receipt names its host, so games check it against the room's host.
  - Only wallets can be dealt to; email sign-ins can't.
  - 30 deals and 120 draws a minute per wallet in one app.
- **UNVERIFIED:** on Postgres under real concurrency (the parallel-draw test ran on PGlite, which serialises), and on a published app in a real browser with a real wallet.

### D142. A crew and saboteur party game template, on the room and the dealer (19 Nov 2026; PR 7 of D139)
- Decided by: the owner (D139's plan). Built by Claude. Original throughout: shown as "Deep Station", an undersea research station with its own tasks, names and look. No other game's name, characters or art; a test checks the files for the best-known one.
- **The game** (`saboteur-party`, 4 to 10 players):
  - Roles come from the dealer (D141): one saboteur for 4 to 6 players, two for 7 to 10. Everyone knows how many, nobody knows who. The host deals once per round and puts the deal id in a signed move. Each player reads only their own role, and trusts only a deal whose receipt names the room's host.
  - **Tasks:** the crew taps numbered tiles in order. Every task done wins it for the crew.
  - **Sabotage:** the oxygen fails, and the crew has 30 s to fix it or the saboteurs win.
  - **Knock-out:** a saboteur knocks someone out, at most every 30 s. That calls a **meeting**: 45 s to talk in the room and vote, or skip. The most votes puts a player out (a tie or skip puts nobody out). Whoever is voted out shows their role.
  - **Endings:** the crew wins when every saboteur is out or every task is done. The saboteurs win when they equal the crew or the oxygen runs out.
- **How it stays fair without a server referee:** knock-outs, sabotage, tasks and fixes go in an `events` collection whose fields are public but whose writer is not (D34 `public` fields with `read: ["writer"]`), so nobody sees who knocked someone out. Votes and shown roles are signed moves. Every screen works out the phase, who is out and who won from the same records with the same code (`station()` in `rules.ts`), using the server's record times.
- **Play the computer:** you and four computer players with a random role. It's shorter (3 tasks each, 10 s meetings), and it's what the run check plays.
- **Preview fix:** the preview's stand-in store now shows another writer's record by its public fields only, as the server does. Before this it showed nothing, so a game using public fields looked empty in the preview.
- **Tests:**
  - The rules on their own: a knock-out too early does nothing; a meeting, the vote and the reveal; a tie; sabotage fixed or not; all tasks done; the saboteurs equalling the crew; the role counts.
  - Four players in a room on the preview: exactly one saboteur is dealt, each player sees only their own role, the knock-out calls a meeting on all four screens, and voting the saboteur out shows "The crew wins" everywhere.
  - The app, type and run checks, with and without MiniPay, plus a slow touch phone and a copy whose task tiles do nothing.
- **Limits:**
  - A role someone shows when they're out is their own word: the dealer keeps hands private and has no reveal. Someone who leaves without showing theirs counts as crew after 10 s.
  - The events' writers are hidden, but a modified client can still send one. That is the same "between friends" limit as every room game.
  - A meeting's clock and the cooldowns are measured on each device against the server's record times, so they can be a few seconds apart.
  - With polling, actions take up to 3 s to reach the other screens.
- **UNVERIFIED:** on a real phone, on a published app with real signatures, and with a real model building from it.

### D143. Card game templates: Solitaire, Higher or lower, Memory pairs and a shedding game on the dealer (19 Nov 2026; PR 8 of D139)
- Decided by: the owner (D139's plan). Built by Claude. Original names and look throughout: GoodBuild's own card face (`src/cards/CardFace.tsx`, a white face, rank and suit sign, the theme's colours). Points only, never money or tokens (D4, D72); a test checks the card games for betting words.
- **Shared:** `src/cards/cards.ts`. Cards are short strings ("QH", "10S"), so they fit the dealer's decks and records unchanged.
- **Solitaire** (`solitaire`, "Patience Lane"): Klondike rules. Seven columns, foundations built up by suit from the ace, the deck turned three at a time, three passes. Tap a card to move it where it can go (a foundation first), which suits a phone. Scoring: 10 a card home, 5 a card turned in a column, 200 for the whole deck. The round ends when the player wins, is stuck, or presses End game. **No leaderboard (changed from the other one-player templates):** a round rarely ends without a player deciding to stop, so the check's bot could never post a score. It keeps the player's best on the device.
- **Higher or lower** (`higher-lower`, "High Card Run"): 3 lives, a streak doubles the points and earns a skip, and the leaderboard is on.
- **Memory pairs** (`memory-pairs`, "Pair Up"): 6 pairs, 16 tries. Points for each card turned for the first time, each pair, and tries left at the end. The leaderboard is on. It is a separate game from the match grid, which clears groups of tiles on a board.
- **Shedding game** (`last-card`, "Last Card Out", 2 to 6): match the top card's suit or rank. An 8 goes on anything and calls the suit. Can't go? Draw one. First with no cards wins, and if the deck is empty and everyone passes, the fewest cards wins.
  - In a room the host deals through the dealer (D141), draws the first card and posts it with the deal id. Each player reads only their own hand (dealt plus drawn, minus what they've played).
  - Plays are public moves, replayed the same way on every screen (`replay` in `rules.ts`), and everyone's card counts come from those moves.
  - Play the computer pits you against two computer players.
- **Tests** (`templates.test.ts`):
  - **The rules on their own:** Solitaire's deal, moves, scoring, passes, stuck and won; the shedding game's turns, wrong cards, the 8, draws, repeats and everyone passing.
  - **Two players in a room on the preview** get 7 cards each with none in common, see the same top card, and a play or draw reaches the other screen and passes the turn.
  - **Every game** passes the app, type and run checks, with and without MiniPay, and plays on a slow touch phone.
  - **A broken copy** of Higher or lower is caught.
- **Limits:** the shedding game shares the room's limits, so a move is a claim between friends. A modified client could play a card it doesn't hold; everyone's counts would show it at the end. Solitaire's tap-to-move picks the first legal column, with no drag to choose another.
- **UNVERIFIED:** on a real phone, on a published app with real signatures, and with a real model building from them.

### D150. Genre templates, part 1: Word hunt, Rhythm lanes and Platform hop (19 Nov 2026; the genre step of D139)
- Decided by: the owner (D139's plan: "more genres"). Built by Claude. Numbered D150 so it can't collide with the other session building D139's PR 5 to PR 8 (room, dealer, saboteur, cards), which agreed to leave the genre templates to this one.
- **Word hunt** (puzzle, letter buttons): today's grid (from `dailySeed`, the same for everyone) hides five words reading across or down; tap their letters, finish a word for a bonus that grows with words in a row, a wrong letter costs one of three lives, a hint lights a letter (another every two words). The word list shows each word's length so the hunt isn't blind. A wrong tap costing a life is what lets a random player (and the run check) finish a round quickly, and a right one scoring is what lets it post a score.
