- **Rhythm lanes** (action, canvas): notes fall on the beat (two beats from top to pad, 100 rising to 170 beats a minute) down four lanes; tap a lane or step with the arrows; each catch plays that lane's note from one scale, so play sounds like a tune. Bombs cost lives, missed notes drain a groove meter, double points and slow time help.
- **Platform hop** (action, canvas): a side-scroller; ledges with gaps, spikes, beetles that walk their ledge (land on one to stomp it), coins, a shield and double points; three lives, a fall puts you back on the next ledge.
- **Found while testing:** a quick tap can start and end between two frames, so `pointer.down` is never seen; Rhythm lanes reads `actionPressed` too (Lane racer already did). Without it the run check reported that the controls never moved the player.
- **Checked:** each passes the app check and the type check, alone and with the MiniPay and board kits; the run check plays each, sees every declared kind, and posts a score; a Word hunt whose letters do nothing and a Rhythm lanes whose pad never moves are caught. Run three times in a row with no failures.
- **Next (part 2):** block stacker, tower defense and an idle clicker. An idle game has no natural end, so its round will be a short shift whose upgrades carry over; this is a proposal until built.
- **UNVERIFIED** with a real model reshaping them, and on a real phone.

## Progressive first delivery and recoverable builds (19 Nov 2026)

The owner approved implementing all five parts of the first-build fix and opening a PR: explicit retry, durable drafts and loop limits, staged MVP delivery, bounded first-build/repair sponsorship, and contract-test readiness. See [PROGRESSIVE_DELIVERY.md](PROGRESSIVE_DELIVERY.md) for schema, funding caps, behavior, validation and rollout limits. The existing mainnet custody gate remains mandatory. Working interface, saved implementation and verified live capability are distinct states. The default sponsorship limits are reviewable launch caps, not measured completion guarantees.

### D151. Genre templates, part 2: Crane stack, Market stall and Path guard (19 Nov 2026; the genre step of D139, after D150)
- Decided by: the owner (D139's plan: "more genres", with an idle clicker, tower defense and a stacker named). Built by Claude.
- **Crane stack** (action, one tap): a block swings on a crane; tap to drop; overhang is cut off, a drop within 5 pixels is perfect (no cut, +25); gusts of wind push the swing and the fall; a wide block and a slow swing help; a miss ends the round.
- **Market stall** (idle and clicker, buttons): 10-second shifts; tap Serve to earn, buy upgrades (more per tap, helpers that earn by themselves, each dearer than the last); a rush doubles takings for 3 seconds once a shift. **The idle part is that upgrades and the till stay on the device between shifts**, so every shift earns more. Rounds that end were needed for the leaderboard and for GoodBuild's check, which is why it isn't an endless idle game.
- **Path guard** (tower defense, one tap): walkers follow a winding path; tap open grass to build a tower (50 coins) that shoots the walker furthest along within range; kills pay; a frost crystal, tapped, slows everything for 4 seconds; waves grow quickly (`difficulty` over 45 seconds) so a round ends in minutes. Ten lives.
- **How the one-tap games are judged:** a game whose player moves by itself (a swinging block) can't show that its controls work by movement alone. Crane stack and Path guard report the place the player last changed (where the last block landed, where the last tower went up), which moves only when the player taps. A copy whose tap does nothing is caught for each.
- **Checked:** each passes the app check and the type check, alone and with the MiniPay and board kits; the run check plays each, sees every declared kind and posts a score; the three broken copies are caught. Three runs in a row with no failures.
- **Thumbnails:** the template picker's art (`public/games/<id>.webp`) exists for the first ten templates only, so the thirteen added on 19 Nov (multiplayer, cards, genres) pointed at missing images. `gameTemplateStarters` now gives a thumbnail only for ids in `GAME_TEMPLATE_ART`, and the picker shows its gamepad icon for the rest; a test checks every listed thumbnail exists. Art for the new ones is still to be made.
- **CI time:** the "Game templates and frame rate" job plays every template one at a time. With 23 templates it ran past the step's 20-minute limit (part 1, with 20, had taken 19.5 minutes), so the step now allows 35 minutes and the job 45 (#148 had raised them to 30 and 35 at the same time; the higher ones were kept, since the step took about 23 minutes). No test was removed or skipped. Splitting the template tests across runners would need the one test file split first.
- **UNVERIFIED** with a real model reshaping them, and on a real phone.

### D152. Harness v2: Sonnet plans and writes contracts, DeepSeek writes the front end, the builder checks its own work (19 Nov 2026; proposed, changes D19)
- Date: 19 Nov 2026 · Proposed by: Claude, at the owner's request · **Decided by: user (Olayinka), in chat on 19 Nov 2026: all five points approved** (the routing change, the higher limits under the flag, the package list, `require` allowed again, acceptance in `warn` first) · Changes D19 (user decision), raises the call limit in [PROGRESSIVE_DELIVERY.md](PROGRESSIVE_DELIVERY.md) under the flag only
- Plan: [HARNESS_V2_PLAN.md](HARNESS_V2_PLAN.md). Everything is behind `HARNESS_V2=off|admins|on`, default `off`; no migration.
- **Routing:** a spec call on `claude-sonnet-5-5` (thinking on) writes the app's plan and acceptance flows to a platform-owned `src/spec.json`. Sonnet writes contracts, tests and `deploy.json`; DeepSeek writes `src/`. The first fix attempt goes to the tier that wrote the failing files; later attempts go to Sonnet as today.
- **Loop (after OpenAI's open-source Codex harness):** the builder runs `run_tests`, `open_app` and `run_acceptance` itself, within a per-turn check-time budget instead of 3 calls; limits are 90 calls and 40 rounds, with spend still bounded by holds, the free ceiling and sponsorship; older file bodies are trimmed from the history at phase boundaries; Sonnet calls cache the tail of the conversation, and cache writes are billed at the provider's rate.
- **Acceptance:** browser flows in a small JSON language, written by the spec call, run by the run check; `ACCEPTANCE=warn` first.
- **Wider box:** about 14 vetted packages, design rules about direction instead of classes, capability kits turned on by the builder rather than by request words, `require(cond, "msg")` allowed again. Every security guardrail stays. D3 (no backend) is unchanged.
- Why: the owner wants apps that work, not template-shaped ones. In the evals, the expensive failures were contract and test code (RTX, E5), which a better plan alone doesn't fix; and the builder couldn't run or see its own work.
- Cost, estimated: about +$0.11 per first build and +$0.30 to $0.40 per contract build before savings from fewer Sonnet fixes and caching. To measure in the canary (HARNESS_V2_PLAN.md).
- Number note: D144 to D149 don't appear in this file; if an open branch uses D152, renumber this one.
- **PR 1 (built, 19 Nov 2026):** the flag, the spec call, the Sonnet contracts phase and the DeepSeek front-end phase, writer-first fixes, `run_tests` and `open_app` with a check-time budget, the v2 limits, history trimmed at phase boundaries, the cached tail of strong calls, cache writes billed, the v2 prompt and design rules, capability kits left to the builder, `require` allowed. Two changes from the plan:
  - No `TURN_SETUP_VERSION` bump: an optional `harness` field on the setup does the job, so turns running during the deploy resume as v1 instead of failing.
  - The upgradeable deploy check also allows `require`, or a v2 contract converted to upgradeable would be refused at deploy.
- **PR 2 (built, 19 Nov 2026): acceptance flows.**
  - `acceptance.ts` holds the flow language (`goto`, `click`, `fill`, `press`, `expect` text/hidden/route/locator, `wait`, `connectWallet`; locators by role and name, text, label, placeholder, testid) and `parseFlows`.
  - The spec call gets a flow that can't run back as an error to fix; a flow with any problem is left out whole.
  - The run check runs each flow on a fresh page in its own context: a stand-in wallet for `wallet` flows, `deployed` flows skipped, a crash during a flow is that flow's failure. Limits: 2 s a step, 20 s a flow, 60 s in all, added to the check's timeout.
  - The builder has `run_acceptance` (optionally one flow by name). The final checks run the spec's flows too.
  - `ACCEPTANCE=warn` (default) logs `acceptance: N of M flows pass` and shows failing flows in `run_checks`' answer. `enforce` adds them as `[acceptance]` issues, so they go to the fix loop and block the milestone's verification.
  - v1 never runs flows.
  - **Live smoke run, DeepSeek only (19 Nov 2026):** the eval suite's `event-rsvp` scenario ($0.108, 289 s, no fix attempts). The spec had 4 pages and 8 acceptance flows, every one of which parsed. The builder opened four pages with `open_app`, ran `run_acceptance` (8 of 8 pass) and `run_checks`, and the final checks logged `acceptance: 8 of 8 flows pass`. Not yet seen live: a failing flow fixed through the loop (covered by the scripted test), and flows written by Sonnet.
- **PR 3 (built, 19 Nov 2026): vetted packages.**
  - Pinned exactly in `V2_PACKAGES` (`template.ts`) and `publish-runtime/package.json`: zustand 5.0.16, immer 11.2.9, zod 4.6.7, react-hook-form 7.91.0, recharts 3.10.1, @dnd-kit/core 6.3.1, @dnd-kit/sortable 10.0.0, matter-js 0.20.0, howler 2.2.4, react-markdown 10.1.0, fuse.js 7.5.0, papaparse 5.7.0, nanoid 6.0.2. Each was the newest stable release published at least two weeks before pinning; all MIT except fuse.js (Apache-2.0).
  - Companions, added with the package because the in-browser preview doesn't install peers: react-is 18.3.1 with recharts; @dnd-kit/core and @dnd-kit/utilities 3.2.2 with sortable (utilities also with core, for `CSS.Transform`). Types for the three without their own (`@types/matter-js`, `@types/howler`, `@types/papaparse`) are platform packages, like `@types/three`.
  - Sizes, minified and gzipped as the publish bundle builds them: zustand 0.4 kB, immer 3.7, zod 91 (4.6 from `zod/mini`, which the guide requires), react-hook-form 12, recharts 111, @dnd-kit 14, matter-js 27, howler 10, react-markdown 36, fuse.js 9, papaparse 7, nanoid 0.2.
  - Only a v2 build may add them (`Workspace` and `packagesFor` take `v2`), and only v2 is told about them: `add_dependency`'s description, one line in the front-end rules and a `packages` guide with an example per package. Publishing accepts them for any project, so a v2 project keeps working if the flag goes off.
  - One change from the plan: the dashboard kit keeps `recharts` in `replaces` (and game-2d keeps matter-js and howler), so a v1 build that imports recharts is still sent to the kit. Under v2 the import check says to call `add_dependency` instead.
  - Found while testing: the starter `Input` doesn't pass refs on, so react-hook-form's `register` gets no value from it. The guide says to register native inputs.
  - **Checked:** `packages.test.ts` builds an app from the guide's examples using every package; it type-checks with no errors against the pinned versions, and in the run check (the publish bundle in headless Chromium) five acceptance flows pass (store, immer, zod/mini, the form's errors and submit, fuzzy search, the list, CSV, nanoid, markdown with raw HTML shown as text). **UNVERIFIED:** loading in the Sandpack preview, since CodeSandbox's package host is blocked from this container; to check in the canary.
- **Review fixes (19 Nov 2026, CodeRabbit on yinkscss/goodbuild#152):**
  - `ACCEPTANCE=enforce` now blocks only on the turn whose spec step wrote the flows. On a later turn they only warn (logged "the spec is from an earlier turn: not blocking") and the builder still sees them, so a request that rewords the page isn't sent back to the old wording. Refreshing the spec on every turn would cost a Sonnet call each time.
  - v2 runs only the tools the current step offers, so `write_spec` outside the spec step is refused. `add_kit`'s starter files pass the phase gates.
  - `expect hidden` fails while any match is visible, not just the first. A failed flow waits at most 3 s for the page outline and the close. A `needs` that isn't one of the three strings, or an `acceptance` that isn't an array, is refused.
  - A spec too long for the context block is shown compact with its flows first and says it was cut off. The fallback price includes the cache-write rate. Rolling back a visual fix restores the browser check's verdict with the files. The import check names the package to add for a companion (`@dnd-kit/core` for `@dnd-kit/utilities`).
  - Not changed: the front end still starts when the contracts phase leaves errors. Those errors reach the fix loop, which has no phase gate and whose first attempt goes to the strong model that wrote the contracts.
- **On for every user (20 Nov 2026, decided by the owner, Olayinka, in chat: "Hard code it on", no environment variable):** `usesHarnessV2()` is always true in production, and `HARNESS_V2` and its `admins` mode are gone. The canary stage of the plan was skipped. A turn saved on v1 before this still resumes on v1, so the v1 loop and its tests stay; tests start on v1 through `setV1HarnessForTests` (test-only) and the v2 tests switch it off. Going back takes a code change.
- **Also changes v1 billing:** cache writes are now billed at Anthropic's write rate (1.25x input) for every Sonnet call, and a paid call's hold allows for them. v1 already cached the system prompt and context, and those writes were billed as plain input before.
- **Checked:** unit and scripted-runner tests (`harness-v2.test.ts`); every existing agent, pricing and credit test passes.
- **Live smoke run, 19 Nov 2026, DeepSeek only:** a local production build with `HARNESS_V2=on`, the run check on and no Anthropic key, so every strong call fell back to `deepseek-flash`. Sonnet itself is still **UNVERIFIED**; that is the owner's canary.
  - Turn 1, the G$ tip jar's interface milestone ($0.086, 238 s): the spec had 2 pages, 0 contracts (the milestone defers them) and 7 acceptance flows. The builder called `open_app` three times by itself, changed files after reading the outline, ran `run_checks` once, and the milestone's browser acceptance passed. No fix attempts.
  - Turn 2, the payments milestone ($0.087, 223 s): the spec named TipJar. The contracts phase wrote the contract, 6 tests and `deploy.json`, fixed two test compile errors through `run_tests`, and reached 6 of 6 passing. The front end read the ABI module, opened the app twice, and fixed 9 type errors that `run_checks` showed it. The turn ended ready with no fix attempts.

### D155. Invite links on the home dashboard; the vote becomes one tap; the invite bonus pays on a confirmed vote (19 Nov 2026; replaces D136's deploy trigger and hackathon-only link)
- Decided by: the owner (a referral link on the user's dashboard that sends friends to verify with GoodDollar and vote; the vote is one tap with no dragging; "Pay on vote" and "All available points" picked from options). Built by Claude.
- **Invite link:** every signed-in person has a code (`users.referral_code`, random, made on first ask, never from the wallet) and a link `/r/CODE`. The home dashboard has an "Invite friends" card (copy, share, and joined / voted / credits earned). The Get started page shows the same card instead of D136's link.
- **Landing `/r/CODE` (works signed out, noindex, 18+ / no US persons notice):** 1 sign in, 2 verify with GoodDollar (returns to the same page, using the lost-hint recovery from the verify button fix), 3 one-tap vote. The code is kept in the browser until sign-in, then `POST /api/referral` records it once. A bad, unknown, own or second code, or someone who has already voted for us, is ignored without an error.
- **One-tap vote (everywhere, slider removed):** one tap switches to Celo, checks eligibility, turns voting on if needed (one signature), prices the vote, gives **all available points** to GoodBuildIt, and confirms it on-chain. Kept: the gas grant, the pending-vote check, "already voted" detection, the Telegram/CELO help. After a smaller earlier vote, "Vote my remaining points" is one more tap.
- **Bonus:** when the invited person's vote is confirmed on-chain, they get 10 credits and the inviter 10 (table `referrals`, migration `0030`). One per GoodDollar identity (unique root), nobody is paid unless the inviter is verified under a different identity (codes are only made for verified people, so a spare account can't invite you to yourself), the inviter is paid for up to 3 friends, both sides together or neither, idempotent ledger keys (`referral:<role>:<root>`). It shares D136's pot (`HACKATHON_REFERRAL_BUDGET_CREDITS`, default 1,000 credits), counted across both tables. A short pot holds the bonus; it is retried only when that person's vote is confirmed again (UNVERIFIED as a gap worth closing: it needs a retry hook). It stacks with the 40 welcome credits (D125).
- **D136:** the hackathon `?ref=` link is no longer captured and no page shows it; referrals already recorded still pay on deploy. Nobody gets both: someone with a D136 row (by account or by GoodDollar identity) is never invited or paid here. The pot is shared, counted across both tables here (D136's own check still counts only its own table, so the two together can overshoot the pot by one bonus at most per race).
- **Not verified in a browser or against live Celo / GoodDollar.** Tests: server rules on PGlite, the vote dialog and landing steps with a mocked wallet.

### D153. Later requests can add and start a milestone; scope refusals say how to proceed and are refunded (19 Nov 2026; fixes progressive delivery)
- Date: 19 Nov 2026 · Decided by: the owner (approved the four-part fix) · Built by: Claude
- **The bug:** the delivery plan (`build_deliveries`) was built once, from the first message. A portfolio site got one milestone, "Working first interface", and kept it for good. Later requests ("add a G$ tip jar contract") ran in that scope, so `milestoneAllows` refused every `contracts/` write. Nothing could add the on-chain milestone, and "continue" offered nothing because the plan said every milestone was complete. The builder only saw "belongs to a later saved milestone", so it told people to turn the on-chain milestone on in project settings. No such setting exists. Each of those replies was charged.
- **Change 1, plans grow:** once the first milestone is completed and no build is running, a build request (or, when a queued message is answered by id, that message's text) is matched against the same milestone words as the first message (`requestedMilestones`). Milestones it asks for that are missing are added to the plan. The first one it asks for that isn't completed yet becomes the turn's milestone, the same way as picking a milestone chip (`extendDelivery`, `startTurn`). A request with none of those words keeps the current scope, as before. Before the first interface is delivered, every build stays the interface.
- **Change 1b, on-chain scope sticks:** once an on-chain (`payments`) milestone has been delivered, contract edits stay allowed in later milestones (for example a wallet milestone added afterwards), and the delivery contract and builder prompt say so.
- **Change 2, honest refusals:** the refusal the builder gets names the current milestone and the way forward: send a request that names the on-chain feature. The system prompt now says, in both modes, that milestones come from the person's messages and that there is no setting for them (`MILESTONE_FACTS`).
- **Change 3, refunds:** a paid build turn that ends with no file changes, after at least one call was refused for milestone scope, gets its charge credited back however it ended (answered, after the checks, or with a provider error) as an `adjust` ledger entry (`refType: scope_refund`, one per job). The usage rows stay as the record of provider cost. The reply shows "Nothing was built, so the … of AI for this reply was refunded." The live view gets it in a snapshot before the turn's done event. If the transaction holding the credit rolls back, the record drops the refund claim. Free, exempt and sponsored turns don't debit the person, so they get nothing back. Questions answered without any refused call are charged as before.
- **Trade-off:** the milestone words are broad (`pool`, `token`, `API`, `email`), so an edit like "make the pool photo bigger" can start the on-chain milestone. That only widens what the builder may write; it doesn't make it write contracts it wasn't asked for, and spending stays behind deploy and the mainnet custody gate. These are the same words the first message has always been matched against.
- **Checked:** unit tests for extending, picking, sticky scope and the refusal text; an end-to-end test where a portfolio project adds a G$ tip jar contract after its first build and the contract write is allowed, an ordinary edit keeps the interface scope, and a refused paid turn is refunded to the same balance. Both end-to-end tests fail against the old code.
- **Not done:** projects already stuck (like the one in the owner's screenshot) recover on their next request that names the feature; nothing is migrated. Credits charged for earlier refused turns are not refunded retroactively. **UNVERIFIED** with a real model.
### D154. Solitaire's run check deals from a fixed seed (19 Nov 2026; fixes a flaky template check)
- Date: 19 Nov 2026 · Decided by: the owner (chose the seeded deal over loosening the score rule) · Built by: Claude
- **The problem:** the run check plays a puzzle game with a bot that taps at random, and reports "the score never went up in 35 moves" if it scores nothing. Solitaire dealt at random, and some deals give no scoring move that early (no ace showing, no column card that can move). In a simulation of the same rules and a random tapper, about 2% of deals failed. That failed CI on #150 (a PR that doesn't touch games), and in production it would send a good Solitaire game into the auto-fix loop.
- **Change:** `gameCheckScript` sets `seed: CHECK_SEED` (18) on `window.__gbGame`; the probe exports `checkSeed()`, undefined for real players; Solitaire deals through `newDeal()`, which shuffles from `seededRandom(seed)` while checked and at random otherwise. Seed 18 deals three aces face up; the simulated bot scored within 35 moves in 20,000 games out of 20,000. The template brief tells the builder to keep every deal going through `newDeal()`.
- **Scope:** only Solitaire uses the seed. Other card and puzzle games keep their own randomness; they can adopt `checkSeed()` if they show the same problem. Real players still get random deals.
- **Checked:** a test that the checked deal is the same every time and shows at least two aces, that the check script carries the seed and that the game deals only through `newDeal()`; the Solitaire run check, type check and rules tests; the game-check, kit and run-check tests. **UNVERIFIED:** the full game-templates CI job, which runs on the PR.

### D156. Celo registry events and a Celoscan link for every deployed contract (19 Nov 2026) — live
- Decided by: the owner (build milestone deliverables 3 and 4, both at 0%). Built by Claude.
- **Why:** both are milestone commitments, so Q11's "DB-only first" and "Celoscan later" no longer hold. Today only `/stats` links mainnet contracts to Celoscan; the deploy modal, interaction panel and published-app notice have no link, and nothing emits registry events.
- **Deliverable 4 (Celoscan link):** link every deployed contract (`celoscan.io/address/<addr>` on 42220, the Celo Sepolia explorer on testnet) in the deploy-success card, contract list/interaction panel and the published app's "Unaudited contract" notice. Then submit source to Celoscan (Etherscan v2 API, needs an API key from the owner), also a MiniPay listing requirement.
- **Deliverable 3 (registry events), revised 19 Nov 2026 after review (owner agreed):** no user-signed "Register on Celo" step. An optional signature would leave the Season 5 counts incomplete, and our users are non-technical. The server writes every event. The deploy itself is already signed on-chain by the user; the registry only indexes it.
  - **Contract** `contracts/GoodBuildRegistry.sol`: events only, no funds, no upgrade path; `recordBatch` (max 100) callable only by a `recorder`; the owner can replace the recorder. Events: `BuilderJoined`, `AppGenerated`, `ProjectDeployed`, `AppPublished`, `AppUnpublished`, `GdollarIntegrated`. Repeat builders are derived off-chain from the events.
  - **Privacy (owner's choice, with a limit to know):** builders and apps are salted hashes (`REGISTRY_SALT`); no wallet, email, user id or project name is written. But `ProjectDeployed` links a builder hash to a contract address, and Celoscan shows that contract's creator wallet, so for anyone who deployed on mainnet the hash is a pseudonym for their wallet, not anonymous. **Owner decided 19 Nov 2026: keep it** (pseudonymous), so repeat builders can be counted. Deployed contracts are already public on Celoscan.
  - **Pipeline:** outbox table `registry_events` (migration 0031, unique `key`); hooks at sign-up, first version, a mainnet deployment (and G$ use when the G$ token is a constructor argument), publish and unpublish never throw; every entry carries `at` (unix seconds; backfill uses each source row's own time, so Season 5 can be told from history); the worker sends pending rows every 2 minutes in one batch, saves the batch's hash the moment it is broadcast and settles it from the receipt later (never resends a slow one), with its own **recorder key** (`RECORDER_KEY_FILE`; installed by `deploy/install-recorder-key.sh` into `/opt/goodbuild-recorder`, mounted in the worker only), separate from the gas wallet so nonces can't collide (owner's choice). Publish fires only when an app goes from not live to live; admin takedowns (set straight in the database) are found by the worker's sweep. Backfill: `scripts/registry-backfill.ts`.
  - **Mainnet only for ProjectDeployed/GdollarIntegrated** (owner's choice). Off until `REGISTRY_SALT` and `REGISTRY_ADDRESS` are set.
  - `/stats` and `/stats.json` show the registry address once `REGISTRY_ADDRESS` is set (the milestone evidence link).
  - **Not done:** deploy the registry to mainnet (needs the owner's go-ahead and a funded recorder key), verify it on Sourcify/Celoscan, run the backfill. **UNVERIFIED** on mainnet. Published apps' "Unaudited contract" notice now links each mainnet contract to Celoscan. Still not done for deliverable 4: Celoscan verification isn't retried if its first try says the contract isn't indexed yet.
- **Milestone wording (GoodBuilders Season 5, Build Milestone 1, due 29 Nov 2026):** "add on-chain event tracking across Season 5. Track builders, generated apps, successful deploys, active apps, G$ integration, and repeat builders." So the registry needs event types for all six: `BuilderJoined`, `AppGenerated`, `ProjectDeployed`, `AppActive` (published), `GdollarIntegrated`, and repeat builders derived from `BuilderJoined`/`ProjectDeployed` by deployer.
- **Recorder wallet (19 Nov 2026):** created on the server, key written straight to `/opt/goodbuild-recorder/recorder_key` (never printed, not in chat). Address `0x30f47589Fcc93A7f4a19B516b8dBf697dB2D3d5a`; the owner funds it with CELO. It is the `recorder` the registry is deployed with.
- **Deployed (19 Nov 2026, owner approved):** `GoodBuildRegistry` on Celo mainnet at `0xfb4b2f5805dc263186eb40cf9ae19200d1cab2a8`, deployed on the server with the recorder wallet paying gas. Owner `0xf044…1531` and recorder `0x30f4…3d5a` read back from the chain. `REGISTRY_ADDRESS` and `REGISTRY_SALT` (random, server only) are set for web and worker. Released in #154 and #158 (the first batch reverted because the server's `recordBatch` ABI lacked `uint64 at`; fixed and a test now compares it with the compiled contract). Backfill run 20 Nov 2026: 82 events sent in two batches (first tx `0x458f6e19…6fbf`). Source verified: Sourcify match, and Celoscan shows the source (v0.8.28). `/stats.json` shows the address.
- **Built (19 Nov 2026, deliverable 4):** the explorer link already existed in the deploy modal, contracts panel and /deployments (celoscan.io on mainnet). New: `src/server/deploy/celoscan.ts` submits mainnet deployments to Celoscan through the Etherscan v2 API (`ETHERSCAN_API_KEY`, set on the server 19 Nov) after Sourcify, as a note under the Sourcify status; it never changes that status. Proxied contracts submit their current code only. **UNVERIFIED** against live Celoscan. Owner chose mainnet only for the registry.
- **Open:** whether GoodDollar counts these events (Q9, UNVERIFIED). Settled: mainnet only; Celoscan key set on the server.
- Claude's input: the registry never gates a deploy, sign-up or publish.

### Ambassador G$ earnings and manual payout requests (20 Nov 2026; extends D155)
- Owner approved 5,000 G$ per qualifying new referral, no referral cap, launch onward. Minimum request is five qualified unpaid referrals (25,000 G$), any day; manual payment target is within 24 hours. The earlier Friday schedule is superseded.
- Existing credit bonuses remain independent. New earnings require server-confirmed GoodDollar identity and settled FlowState support, one reward per identity. Identities recorded before attribution do not count. AMBASSADOR_START controls launch; old referrals are not backfilled into earnings.
- `/home/referrals` shows anonymous referral progress, available/requested/paid G$, receiving wallet, requests and payment links. `/home/referrals/admin` uses ADMIN_WALLETS for evidence review and manual payout reconciliation.
- Payout requests freeze the entire available balance, wallet and earning allocations. One open request per ambassador. Processing requires manual payment; the recorded transfer must match the official Celo G$ contract, approved sender, recipient, exact amount and 30-block finality. A transaction log cannot pay two requests. Audit records retain admin transitions and reasons.
- No automatic transfers or payout keys. Set AMBASSADOR_PAYOUT_WALLETS before confirming receipts. Dashboard refresh repairs interrupted qualification from stored voter records with fresh chain checks. Live payout/browser verification is still required before launch.

### D157. Mainnet deploys: the gas wallet silently covers the network fee, a few a day (19 Nov 2026; completes D124's deploy path)
- Decided by: the owner (keep the vote grant at 0.3 CELO; pay the fee at deploy time instead; no message to the user; 10 a day for people who have bought credits). The other numbers below are Claude's defaults, for the owner to change.
- **What:** when a GoodDollar-verified person prepares a Celo mainnet deploy and their wallet can't pay the estimated fee, `POST /api/projects/[id]/deploy/prepare` tops the wallet up to that estimate from the gas wallet before returning the plan. **The person still signs and sends every deploy themselves** (D2, CLAUDE.md rule 8): GoodBuild only sends CELO and never holds a key or sends a deploy. **Silent:** no new text in the modal. If a top-up can't happen (not verified, off, empty, limit reached, too dear), prepare carries on as before and the modal's existing "add CELO" help applies. It never fails a prepare.
- **Limits (`src/server/gas-grant/deploy-grant.ts`):** per person (GoodID root, so extra accounts don't help) per UTC day: **3**, or **10** for anyone with an on-chain credits purchase (ledger deposit with ref type `onchain`; free, welcome and referral credits don't count). The count resets at midnight UTC and doesn't accumulate. At most **3 CELO** per deploy (a bigger plan isn't covered at all, so nothing is half-funded) and **20 CELO a day** over everyone, separate from the vote's 50 × 0.3. The same plan on the same day is one grant, so re-opening the modal can't be farmed.
- **Estimate (UNVERIFIED for big or multi-contract plans):** 150,000 gas plus 230 gas per byte for each contract still to deploy (a first upgradeable deploy adds its proxy), times the fee cap (twice the base fee plus tip), plus 20%. The one measured point is 479,062 gas for 2,157 bytes (MAINNET_PLAN section 7). The wallet needs the fee cap, not the fee, in its balance, so people keep up to about half of what they're sent. A bad estimate means the deploy asks for a little CELO as before; it costs the owner at most the per-deploy ceiling.
- **Shared with the vote grant:** same table, lock and nonce sequence, so the gas wallet never signs two transactions with one nonce (migration 0032: one-per-person is now a partial unique index on vote rows, plus a `plan_key` column). Two switches: `gas_grants` (everything) and `gas_deploy_grants` (deploys only), e.g. `insert into app_settings (key, value) values ('gas_deploy_grants', 'off') on conflict (key) do update set value = 'off';`. A missing row means on. Admin page: a "Deploy fees today" tile.
- **If the deploy then fails:** the CELO stays in their wallet and the grant counts for the day.
- **Credits code:** deploy-grant.ts only *reads* whether a person has bought credits. Nothing moves between credits and gas, and the test that bans importing credits code in this folder still passes.
- **Not done / UNVERIFIED:** a live mainnet deploy through this path (needs the owner's funded gas wallet, `deploy/install-gas-key.sh` and migration 0032 applied); whether Magic email wallets show the topped-up balance in time (it waits up to 20 s for the transfer to land); the estimate against real multi-contract deploys.
